along

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes V\boxtimes U,s\times s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\}),0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{1\boxtimes U,s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes V\boxtimes U,s\times s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc},W,0}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\operatorname{id}}

Moreover,
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}
is the
\diamond\diamond
-analytification of the corresponding truncated Witt vector version as considered in

[
XZ17
, example 6.1.9]
.

Proof.

We use the truncated version of Shtukas and Hecke stacks as defined in
[
XZ17
, section 5.3]
, and recalled in appendix

. The truncated perfect stacks as defined in appendix
A.3
can be defined directly in the world of v-stacks, either by writing out the obvious moduli problem, or taking the corresponding
\diamond\diamond
-analytification.

We note that the truncated version of diagram (
29
) is really the
\diamond\diamond
-analytification of the diagram below of perfect stacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*}\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m),0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc}(m)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*}\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),0}_{V^{*}\boxtimes V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}_{V^{*}\boxtimes V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\operatorname{loc}(m)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\operatorname{loc}(m)}}

which defines

by pullback of
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc}(m),+,0}
. We know that

so it follows from proposition

that

\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}=\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),0,\diamond\diamond}

under the identification in appendix
A.3
.

∎

5.1.2.
The special fiber

Lastly, we identify the creation correspondence at the special fiber. We want to prove that the pullback of
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}
(restricted on the special fiber) along the restriction map

\operatorname{Sht}^{W}_{U}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sht}_{U,s}
is precisely the
\diamond\diamond
-analytification of the creation correspondence constructed in
[

To simplify the notation, we denote

\operatorname{Sht}_{U|V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}^{(\{1\})|(\{1,2\},\{3\}),0}:=\operatorname{Sht}_{U|V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s|s\times s\times s}^{(\{1\})|(\{1,2\},\{3\}),0},

and

Similarly, we denote

and

\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})}:=\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s\times s\times s}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})}.

As before, there is the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{0,W}_{\mu_{\bullet}|\mu_{\bullet}^{\prime}}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{U}^{(\{1\})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})}}

and we want to understand the pullback of the (restriction to the special fiber of)
creation correspondence
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}
in (
25
) along the upper half of the diagram.
We note that the diagram factorizes as

(31)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{0,W}_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{0,W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}^{(\{1\})|(\{1,2\},\{3\}),0}_{U|V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,s}^{(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{U}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{(U|V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}}

where the vertical maps of the second row are restriction of torsors from
W(R^{+})
to
\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(R,R^{+})
.

We can pullback the cohomological correspondence
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{+}
(restricted to the special fiber) defined in example
3.10
(
11
) along the last diagram, which defines the creation correspondence on the global Witt vector shtuka

(32)

\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{W}:(\operatorname{Sht}_{U}^{W},\epsilon_{U}^{W,*}r_{U}^{*}\mathscr{S}_{U}^{(\{1\})})\longrightarrow(\operatorname{Sht}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W},(\epsilon_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W})^{*}(r_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{(\{1\},\{2\})}).

The importance of
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{W}

is that it is the
\diamond\diamond
-analytification of the creation correspondence on moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas constructed in
[
XZ17
, Example 6.1.9]
.

Proposition 5.6
.

\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{W}
is the pullback of
the restriction of the creation correspondence
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{+}
in (
25
) to
\operatorname{Spd}(\overline{\mathbb{F}_{p}})

along

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{0,W}_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U|V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}^{(\{1\})|(\{1,2\},\{3\}),0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U,s}^{(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})}.}

Moreover, we have

\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{W}=\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond},

the analytification of the creation correspondence in
[
XZ17
, Example 6.1.9]
.

Proof.

The factorization (
30
) tells us that
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{W}
is the pullback of the creation correspondence (
25
) along

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{0,W}_{\mu|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U|V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}^{(\{1\})|(\{1,2\},\{3\}),0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U,s}^{(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})}.}

Next, we observe that the restriction of
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{+}
, as defined in example
3.10
(
11
), to the special fiber has a local counter part
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc},+}
, and
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{+}
is the pullback of
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc},+}
along the canonical restriction diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}^{(\{1\})|(\{1,2\},\{3\}),0}_{U|V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,s}^{(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})|(\{1,2\},\{3\}),0}_{U|V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,s}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1,2\},\{3\})}.}

The restriction to the special fiber is important here, as otherwise
\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})|(\{1,2\},\{3\}),0}_{U|V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}
does not exist, see remark
2.7
. As always,
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{+}
being the pullback of
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc},+}

follows ultimately from 6) of theorem
3.5
. Now
we can compose the diagram (
31
) with the above restriction diagram, and obtain

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{0,W}_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{0,W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})|(\{1,2\},\{3\})0}_{U|V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,s}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1,2\},\{3\})},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{U}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W}}

and
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{W}
is the pullback of
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc},+}
along the diagram.
Up to truncation, this is precisely

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{0,\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond}_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{0,\operatorname{loc}(m),\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc}(m),\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m),\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}^{0,\operatorname{loc}(m),\diamond\diamond}_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc}(m),\diamond\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m),\diamond\diamond},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{U}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{\operatorname{Hecke}_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{0,\operatorname{loc}(m)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{\operatorname{Hecke}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc}(m)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{\operatorname{Hecke}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m)}}}

see appendix
A.1
for the notation of
a_{?}
, and
A.3
for the truncated Witt vector Hecke stacks and moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas. We know from appendix
A.3
that the truncated version does not affect cohomology, so we can naturally view the cohomological correspondences on the (analytification of) truncated Witt vector Hecke stacks or moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas as being defined on the untruncated ones.

We can perform the same construction as of
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{+}
(in the theory of perfect stacks) to obtain

\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc}(m),+}:(\operatorname{Hecke}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc}(m)},IC_{U})\longrightarrow(\operatorname{Hecke}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m)},IC_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V})

supported on

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}^{0,\operatorname{loc}(m)}_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc}(m)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m)},}

which is precisely the creation correspondence constructed in
[
XZ17
, Example 6.1.9]
. Now 6) of theorem
3.5
(and the comparison results in appendices
A.1
and
A.2
) tells us that

\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc}(m),+,\diamond\diamond}=\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc},+}

under the identification of sheaves on truncated and untruncated Hecke stacks in
A.3
. By definition of
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc}(m),+,\diamond}
,
see appendix
A.2
, and
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{+}
being the pullback of
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc},+}
, we have

\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc}(m),+,\diamond}=\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{+}.

Then the commutation between the analytification and pullback of cohomological correspondences (proposition
A.4
) tells us that

\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond}=\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{W}

again under the identification of sheaves on truncated and untruncated Witt vector Hecke stacks and moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas, where

\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}

is the pullback of
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc}(m)}

along

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{0,\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{0,\operatorname{loc}(m)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc}(m)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{U|(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{U}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}}

As usual, we use
\epsilon_{?}
to denote the (in the sense of perfect stack) forgetting map (viewing the Frobenius twist of
\mathcal{P}_{1}
as
\mathcal{P}_{2}
).
\mathscr{S}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}
is exactly the construction of the creation correspondence on moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas in
[
XZ17
, Example 6.1.9]
, so we have proved that
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{W}
is the analytification of the creation correspondence in
loc.cit
.
∎

5.2.
The annihilation correspondences

Dually, we have the annihilation correspondences.

Let
\zeta:\{1\}\longrightarrow\{1,2,3\}
be a map defined by
\zeta(1)=3
,
and

\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U|U,s\times s\times-|-}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})|(\{1\}),0}\subset\operatorname{Sht}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})|(\{1\}),0}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U|U}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{3}\times\operatorname{Spd}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),\operatorname{id}\times\zeta}\operatorname{Spd}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{3}

be the v-substack parametrizing modifications

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}}

with the first line in

\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times-}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}:=\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}|_{\operatorname{Spd}(\overline{\mathbb{F}_{p}})\times\operatorname{Spd}(\overline{\mathbb{F}_{p}})\times\operatorname{Spd}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})},

and the second line in
\operatorname{Sht}_{U}^{(\{1\})}
. Moreover, the legs indexed by
\mu
on the first and the second row are the same.

Similarly, let

\operatorname{Hecke}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U|U,s\times s\times-|-}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})|(\{1\}),0}\subset\operatorname{Sht}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})|(\{1\}),0}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U|U}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{3}\times\operatorname{Spd}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),\operatorname{id}\times\zeta}\operatorname{Spd}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{3}

be the v-substack parametrizing modifications

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}}

with the first line in

\operatorname{Hecke}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times-}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}:=\operatorname{Hecke}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}|_{\operatorname{Spd}(\overline{\mathbb{F}_{p}})\times\operatorname{Spd}(\overline{\mathbb{F}_{p}})\times\operatorname{Spd}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})},

and the second line in
\operatorname{Hecke}_{U}^{(\{1\})}
. Moreover, the legs indexed by
U
on the first and the second row are the same.

The annihilation correspondence is defined to be the cohomological correspondence

(33)

\mathscr{C}_{\flat}:(\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times-}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})},\epsilon_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})})\longrightarrow(\operatorname{Sht}_{U}^{(\{1\})},\epsilon_{\mu}^{(\{1\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{U}^{(\{1\})})

supported on

\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U|U,s\times s\times-|-}^{0}
that is the pullback
of

(34)

\mathscr{C}_{\flat}^{+}:(\operatorname{Hecke}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times-}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})},\mathscr{S}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})})\longrightarrow(\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu}^{(\{1\})},\mathscr{S}_{U}^{(\{1\})})

defined in example
3.11

along

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U|U,s\times s\times-|-}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})|(\{1\}),0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times-}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U}^{(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})|(\{1\}),0}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U|U,s\times s\times-|-}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times-}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U}^{(\{1\})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U|U}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})|(\{1\})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{U}^{(\{1\})}}

5.2.1.
The generic fiber

We can identify the generic fiber of the annihilation correspondence, which is

(35)

\mathscr{C}_{\flat,\eta}:p_{1}^{*}\epsilon^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\}),*}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U}\mathscr{S}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})}\longrightarrow p_{2}^{!}\epsilon_{U}^{(\{1\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{U}^{(\{1\})}

supported on

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\}),0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{1\boxtimes U,s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}

As before, we have the local Witt vector version, i.e. the cohomological correspondence

\mathscr{C}_{\flat}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}:p_{1}^{*}\epsilon^{\operatorname{loc},W,*}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}\mathscr{S}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\},\{2\})}\longrightarrow p_{2}^{!}\epsilon^{\operatorname{loc},W,*}_{1}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}

supported on

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}^{\operatorname{loc},W,0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}^{\operatorname{loc},W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}^{\operatorname{loc}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{\operatorname{loc}}}

corresponding to the evaluation map

V\otimes V^{*}\longrightarrow 1.

Proposition 5.7
.

The annihilation cohomological correspondence
\mathscr{C}_{\flat}
of (
35
) is naturally identified with the pullback of

\mathscr{C}_{\flat}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}\boxtimes\operatorname{id}:(\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})},(\epsilon_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}^{\operatorname{loc},W,*}\mathscr{S}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\},\{2\})})\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})})\longrightarrow

(\operatorname{Sht}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})},(\epsilon_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},W,*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})})\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})})

along the restriction diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\}),0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{1\boxtimes U,s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}^{\operatorname{loc},W,0}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\operatorname{id}}

Moreover,
\mathscr{C}_{\flat}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}
is the
\diamond\diamond
-analytification of the
corresponding truncated Witt vector version
\mathscr{C}_{\flat}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),0}
constructed in
[
XZ17
, Example 6.1.9]
.

Proof.

Same as the proof of proposition
5.5
.
∎

5.2.2.
The special fiber

We can identify the annihilation correspondence at the special fiber as well.

Proposition 5.8
.

\mathscr{C}_{\flat}^{W}
is the pullback of the restriction of the annihilation correspondence (
33
) to
\operatorname{Spd}(\overline{\mathbb{F}_{p}})

along the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{0,W}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)|U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U|U,s}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})|(\{1\}),0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U,s}^{(\{1\})}}

where

(36)

\mathscr{C}_{\flat}^{W}:(\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)}^{W},(\epsilon^{W}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)})^{*}(r_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)}^{(\{1\},\{2\})})\longrightarrow(\operatorname{Sht}_{U}^{W},(\epsilon_{U}^{W})^{*}r_{U}^{*}\mathscr{S}_{U}^{(\{1\})})

is the pullback of (
34
) (restricted to
\operatorname{Spd}(\overline{\mathbb{F}_{p}})
) along

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)|U}^{0,W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)|U}^{0,W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}^{(\{1\},\{2\})|(\{1\}),0}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)|U,s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U),s}^{(\{1\},\{2\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,s}^{(\{1\})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon^{W}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)|U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon^{W}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{U}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)|U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{U}}

Moreover,
\mathscr{C}_{\flat}^{W}
is the analytification of the annihilation correspondence in
[
XZ17
, Example 6.1.9]
, i.e.

\mathscr{C}_{\flat}^{W}=\mathscr{C}_{\flat}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond}

under the identification in
A.2
between sheaves on truncated and untruncated moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas.

Proof.

Same as the proof of proposition
5.6
.
∎

5.3.
The partial Frobenius

Lastly, we define the partial Frobenius morphism. Let
\boxtimes U_{i}
be a representation of
\hat{G}^{I}
, with each
U_{i}
a representation of
\hat{G}^{I_{i}}
. Moreover, we assume that

U_{1}
is a representation of
(^{L}G)^{I_{1}}
, so
U_{1}
is isomorphic to its Frobenius twist
{}^{\sigma}U_{1}
, and we fix such an isomorphism from now on.

We define the partial Frobenius to be the map

F:\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})}

sending

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{k}}

to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{2}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\varphi_{1}}

Note that we have used the assumption on
U_{1}
so that
\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\varphi_{1}
is still bounded by
U_{1}
. In general,
\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\varphi_{1}

would be bounded by
{}^{\sigma}U_{1}
.

We have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}^{(I_{1})}_{U_{1}}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}^{(I_{1})}_{U_{1}}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}_{(I_{1},I_{2}\cup\cdots\cup I_{k})}\circ\epsilon^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{(I_{2}\cup\cdots\cup I_{k},I_{1})}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})}\circ\epsilon^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}\times\text{id}}

We have a canonical isomorphism

\mathscr{C}_{F}^{+}:\operatorname{Frob}^{*}\mathscr{S}_{U_{1}}^{(I_{1})}\cong\mathscr{S}_{U_{1}}^{(I_{1})},

from which we obtain

F^{*}\epsilon^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1}),*}\mathscr{S}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})}\cong F^{*}\epsilon^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1}),*}\kappa_{(I_{2}\cup\cdots\cup I_{k},I_{1})}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1}),*}(\mathscr{S}_{U_{1}}^{(I_{1})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k})})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\epsilon^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k}),*}\kappa^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k}),*}_{(I_{1},I_{2}\cup\cdots\cup I_{k})}(\operatorname{Frob}\times\text{id})^{*}(\mathscr{S}_{U_{1}}^{(I_{1})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k})})

\displaystyle\overset{\mathscr{C}_{F}^{+}\boxtimes\text{id}}{\cong}

\displaystyle\epsilon^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k}),*}\kappa^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k}),*}_{(I_{1},I_{2}\cup\cdots\cup I_{k})}(\mathscr{S}_{U_{1}}^{(I_{1})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k})})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\epsilon^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k}),*}\mathscr{S}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})},

where the first and the last isomorphism are 4) of theorem
3.5
.
It defines a pullback cohomological correspondence

\mathscr{C}_{F}:(\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})},\epsilon_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1}),*}\mathscr{S}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})})\longrightarrow(\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})},\epsilon_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k}),*}\mathscr{S}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})})

supported on

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

Similarly, we have partial Frobenius on moduli spaces of local Witt vector shtukas,

F^{\operatorname{loc},W}:\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{\operatorname{loc},W}

sending

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{k}}

to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{2},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\varphi_{1}}

where we view each
U_{i}
as a representation of
\hat{G}
through the diagonal embedding
\hat{G}\hookrightarrow\hat{G}^{I_{i}}
.

There is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},W}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},W}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U_{1},s}^{\operatorname{loc},(I_{1})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k},s}^{\operatorname{loc},(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U_{1},s}^{\operatorname{loc},(I_{1})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k},s}^{\operatorname{loc},(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\operatorname{loc},W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{(I_{1},I_{2}\cup\cdots\cup I_{k})}^{\operatorname{loc},(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}\circ\epsilon_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{\operatorname{loc},W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{(I_{2}\cup\cdots\cup I_{k},I_{1})}^{\operatorname{loc},(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}\circ\epsilon_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{\operatorname{loc},W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}\times\text{id}}

As for the case of moduli space of shtukas, the diagram induces a pullback cohomological correspondence

\mathscr{C}_{F}^{\operatorname{loc},W}:F^{\operatorname{loc},W*}\epsilon_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{\operatorname{loc},W,*}\mathscr{S}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{\operatorname{loc},(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})}\cong\epsilon_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{\operatorname{loc},W,*}\mathscr{S}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{\operatorname{loc},(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}

supported on

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},W}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},W}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},W}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\operatorname{loc},W}}

which is the
\diamond\diamond
-analytification of the partial Frobenius correspondence constructed in
[
XZ17
, Lemma 6.1.11]
, namely,

\mathscr{C}_{F}^{W}=\mathscr{C}_{F}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond\diamond}

under the identification in
A.3
between sheaves on truncated and untruncated moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas,
where
\mathscr{C}_{F}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}
is the partial Frobenius cohomological correspondence considered in
[
XZ17
, Lemma 6.1.11]
. Note that the inverse of the partial Frobenius is used in
loc.cit.
as it is better suited with the truncated moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas, after passing to the analytic untruncated version it agrees with our construction.

We now specialize to the situation that is relevant to us.

Example 5.9
.

We consider

F:\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times-}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s\times-\times s}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})}.

Note that we have used our assumption that
V
is a representation of
{}^{L}G
here. We have the partial Frobenius cohomological correspondence

(37)

\mathscr{C}_{F}:(\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s\times-\times s}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})},\epsilon_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})})\longrightarrow(\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times-}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})},\epsilon_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})})

supported on

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times-}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s\times-\times s}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times-}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

If we restrict to the generic fiber, then under the identification in lemma
5.2
, we have

\mathscr{C}_{F,\eta}:(\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s\times\eta\times s}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})},\epsilon_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})})\longrightarrow(\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})},\epsilon_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})})

supported on

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s\times\eta\times s}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

We want to relate it with the local Witt vector partial Frobenius correspondence

(38)

\mathscr{C}_{F}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}:F^{\operatorname{loc},W*}\epsilon_{V^{*}\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc},W,*}\mathscr{S}_{V^{*}\boxtimes V}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\},\{2\})}\cong\epsilon_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}^{\operatorname{loc},W,*}\mathscr{S}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\},\{2\})}

supported on

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},W}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},W}_{V^{*}\boxtimes V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},W}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\operatorname{loc},W}}

As usual, this is the
\diamond\diamond
-analytification of the corresponding truncated Witt vector version
\mathscr{C}_{F}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),0}
.

Proposition 5.10
.

The cohomological correspondence

\mathscr{C}_{F,\eta}
is the same as the pullback of
\mathscr{C}_{F}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}\boxtimes\operatorname{id}

along the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s\times\eta\times s}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},W}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},W}_{V^{*}\boxtimes V}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{id}}

where the vertical arrows are as before restriction maps.

Moreover,

\mathscr{C}_{F}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}=\mathscr{C}_{F}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),0,\diamond\diamond},

being the
\diamond\diamond
-analytification of the truncated Witt vector version considered in
[
XZ17
, Lemma 6.1.11]
.

Proof.

It is clear that the two squares in the diagram are Cartesian, so we can pullback cohomological correspondences. We have the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s\times\eta\times s}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V,s}^{(\{1\})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times\eta}^{(\{1,2\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V,s}^{(\{1\})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times\eta}^{(\{1,2\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V,s}^{(\{1\})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*},s}^{(\{1\})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V,s}^{(\{1\})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*},s}^{(\{1\})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V,s}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*},s}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V,s}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*},s}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}\circ\epsilon_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{(\{1,2\},\{3\})}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})}\circ\epsilon_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}\times\text{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{id}\times\kappa_{(\{1\},\{2\})}^{(\{1\},\{2\})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{id}\times\kappa_{(\{1\},\{2\})}^{(\{1\},\{2\})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}\times\text{id}\times\text{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}\times\text{id}\times\text{id}}

where the bottom vertical maps are global to local restriction maps.
We observe that it
factorizes through

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s\times s\times\eta}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s\times\eta\times s}^{(\{1\},\{2\},\{3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},W}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},W}_{V^{*}\boxtimes V}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V,s}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*},s}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{V,s}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{V^{*},s}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\text{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{(\{1\},\{2\})}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\},\{2\})}\circ\epsilon_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\text{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{(\{1\},\{2\})}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\},\{2\})}\circ\epsilon_{V\boxtimes V^{*}}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\text{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}\times\text{id}\times\text{id}}

where the top vertical arrows are restriction maps,
and the proposition follows from the construction of
\mathscr{C}_{F}
and
\mathscr{C}_{F}^{\operatorname{loc},W}
. Note that in the construction of
\mathscr{C}_{F}
, we can also use the local Hecke stacks.
∎

Lastly, we want to identify the special fiber of the partial Frobenius
\mathscr{C}_{F}
, i.e.

\mathscr{C}_{F,s}:(\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1},s}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})},\epsilon_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1}),*}\mathscr{S}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})})\longrightarrow(\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k},s}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})},\epsilon_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k}),*}\mathscr{S}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})})

supported on

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k},s}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1},s}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k},s}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

We first define the partial Frobenius on moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas. Similar to the other two cases, it is defined to be

F^{W}:\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{W}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{W}

sending

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{k}}

to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{2}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\varphi_{1}}

We have the commutative diagram

(39)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k},s}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k},s}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{W_{1}}^{(I_{1})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{W_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes W_{k},s}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{W_{1}}^{(I_{1})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{W_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes W_{k},s}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{(I_{1},I_{2}\cup\cdots\cup I_{k})}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}\circ\epsilon_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{(I_{2}\cup\cdots\cup I_{k},I_{1})}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})}\circ\epsilon_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}\times\text{id}}

which factorizes as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U_{1}}^{W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U_{1}}^{W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U_{1},s}^{(I_{1})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k},s}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{U_{1},s}^{(I_{1})}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k},s}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{(I_{1},I_{2}\cup\cdots\cup I_{k})}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k}),W}\circ\epsilon_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{(I_{2}\cup\cdots\cup I_{k},I_{1})}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1}),W}\circ\epsilon_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}\times\text{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{U_{1}}\times r_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{U_{1}}\times r_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}\times\text{id}}

The last diagram defines the Witt vector partial Frobenius correspondence

\mathscr{C}_{F}^{W}:F^{W,*}\epsilon_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}\boxtimes U_{1}}^{W,*}\kappa_{(I_{2}\cup\cdots\cup I_{k},I_{1})}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k},I_{1}),W,*}(r_{U_{1}}\times r_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{U_{1}}^{(I_{1})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k})}

\cong\epsilon_{U_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{W,*}\kappa_{(I_{1},I_{2}\cup\cdots\cup I_{k})}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k}),W,*}(r_{U_{1}}\times r_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{U_{1}}^{(I_{1})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U_{2}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes U_{k}}^{(I_{2},\cdots,I_{k})},

which, similar as before, is the
\diamond
-analytification of the partial Frobenius correspondence constructed in
[
XZ17
, Lemma 6.1.11]
.

Now the diagram (
39
) tells us that
\mathscr{C}_{F}^{W}
is the pullback of
\mathscr{C}_{F,s}

along the first square of (
39
). Specializing to the case of our interest, we have

Proposition 5.11
.

The correspondence

(40)

\mathscr{C}_{F}^{W}:F^{W,*}\epsilon_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W,*}\kappa_{(\{1\},\{2\})}^{(\{1\},\{2\}),W,*}(r_{V}\times r_{(V^{*}\otimes U)})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{(\{1\},\{2\})}

\cong\epsilon_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)}^{W,*}\kappa_{(\{1\},\{2\})}^{(\{1\},\{2\}),W,*}(r_{V}\times r_{(V^{*}\otimes U)})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)}^{(\{1\},\{2\})}

supported on

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{W}}

is the pullback of

\mathscr{C}_{F,s}

along the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{(V^{*}\otimes U)\boxtimes V}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes(V^{*}\otimes U)}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V,s}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U,s}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

Moreover,
\mathscr{C}_{F}^{W}
is the
\diamond
-analytification of the partial Frobenius cohomological correspondence considered in
[
XZ17
, Lemma 6.1.11]
.

5.4.
The excursion operators

We can now define the excursion operator associated to
V
as the cohomological correspondence

S_{V}:=\mathscr{C}_{\flat}\circ\mathscr{C}_{F}\circ\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}

from

(\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu},\epsilon_{\mu}^{(\{1\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{U}^{(\{1\})})

to itself supported on

\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu}^{V}:=\underset{\nu}{\bigcup}\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu}^{\nu},

where
\nu
ranges over highest weights of irreducible components of
V
.
Indeed, we have

Lemma 5.12
.

The support of
S_{V}
is
\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu}^{V}
.

Proof.

By definition, the support of
S_{V}
is the fiber product of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U|U}^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{U|V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V}^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U}^{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V}^{(\{1,2\},\{3\})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

which by definition parametrizes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\varphi_{1}}

where the upper left triangle lies in

\operatorname{Sht}_{V\boxtimes V^{*}\boxtimes U|U}^{0}
,
and the lower right triangle lies in

\operatorname{Sht}_{U|V^{*}\boxtimes U\boxtimes V}^{0}
. In other words,
\varphi_{1}
has a leg at the characteristic
p
untilt and is bounded by
V
, the two horizontal modifications have legs varying in
\operatorname{Spd}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
and is bounded by
U
, while the diagonal one has two legs, with one fixed at characteristic
p
untilt bounded by
V^{*}
, and the other varying in
\operatorname{Spd}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
bounded by
U
.
This is then the same as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\varphi_{1}}

with the two lines lying in
\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu}
and
\varphi_{1}
bounded by
V
,
which is nothing but what

\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu}^{V}
parametrizes. Indeed, the diagonal is just
\psi\circ\varphi_{1}^{-1}
, and the modification is taking place at the legs of
\varphi_{1}
and
\psi
, bounded by the corresponding cocharcaters of
\varphi_{1}^{-1}
and
\psi
.
∎

Note that we have

\operatorname{Sht}^{V}_{\mu|\mu,\eta}=\Gamma_{V},

thus
S_{V}
restricted on
\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,\eta}
is supported on
\Gamma_{V}
, the same as the support of the Hecke correspondence.

Similarly, we have the local Witt vector version cohomological correspondence

S_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}:=\mathscr{C}_{\flat}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}\circ\mathscr{C}_{F}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}\circ\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}

from

(\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W},\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})})

to itself supported on
\Gamma_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}
, which can be identified with
\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},V,W}_{0|0}
. Indeed, similar to the proof of previous lemma, we have that the support of
S_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}
parametrizes diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

which is nothing but

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{2}\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\varphi_{1})\varphi_{2}}

whence

\Gamma_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}=\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},V,W}_{0|0}
.

Theorem 5.13
.

The cohomological correspondence
S_{V,\eta}
, the restriction of
S_{V}
on
\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,\eta}
, is the pullback of

S_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}\boxtimes\operatorname{id}:(\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})},(\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W,*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})})\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})})\longrightarrow

(\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})},(\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W,*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})})\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})})

along the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\operatorname{id}}

where the vertical arrows are restriction maps, in particular, the middle one sends

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{2}}

to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}|_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}^{\wedge}_{\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(S),S})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}|_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}^{\wedge}_{\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(S),S})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma|_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}^{\wedge}_{\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(S),S})}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}|_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}^{\wedge}_{\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(S),S^{\sharp}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}|_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}^{\wedge}_{\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(S),S^{\sharp}})}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}

Moreover,
S_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}
is the
\diamond\diamond
-analytification of

S_{V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),0}:=\mathscr{C}_{\flat}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n,0)}\circ\mathscr{C}_{F}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n,0)}\circ\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n,0)},

the truncated Witt vector excursion operator in
[
XZ17
, Theorem 6.0.1 (1)]
.

Proof.

Since we know that pullback of cohomological correspondences respects composition, the theorem follows from proposition
5.5
,
5.7
and
5.10
.
∎

Remark 5.14
.

The theorem holds with
\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,\eta}
replaced by arbitary
\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu_{\bullet},\eta}^{(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})}
. The proof goes without change. We state it only for shtukas with a single leg to simplify the notation and that is the only case matters for Shimura varieties.

Lastly, we want to study the excursion operators at the special fiber. We have the global Witt vector excursion operator, which is the composition of (
32
), (
40
) and (
36
), i.e.

S_{V}^{W}:=\mathscr{C}_{\flat}^{W}\circ\mathscr{C}_{F}^{W}\circ\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{W}

is the cohomological correspondence from

(\operatorname{Sht}^{W}_{\mu},\epsilon_{\mu}^{W,*}r_{\mu}^{*}\mathscr{S}_{U}^{(\{1\})})

to itself supported on

\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu}^{V,W}
.

Proposition 5.15
.

S_{V}^{W}
is the pullback of
S_{V,s}
along

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu}^{V,W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu,s}^{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,s}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}

Moreover,
S_{V}^{W}
is the analytification of the truncated Witt vector excursion operator considered in
[
XZ17
]
, under the equivalence between sheaves on truncatd and untruncated moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas in
A.3
.

Proof.

As
\mathscr{C}_{\sharp}^{W}
,
\mathscr{C}_{F}^{W}
and
\mathscr{C}_{\flat}^{W}
are all analytification of the truncated Witt vector counter part considered in
[
XZ17
]
, so does the composite
S_{V}^{W}
by proposition
A.4
. Then proposition
5.11
, proposition
5.8
and proposition
5.6
tells us that
S_{V}^{W}
is the pullback of
S_{V,s}
, the restriction of
S_{V}
to the special fiber.
∎

6.
S=T
for moduli spaces of shtukas

Let us first look more closely at
S_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}
. Recall that it is supported on
\Gamma_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}=\operatorname{Hecke}_{V}^{\operatorname{loc}}=L^{+,W}\mathcal{G}\setminus\text{Gr}^{W}_{G,\leq V}
, where
L^{+,W}\mathcal{G}
is the v-sheaf whose value at a characteristic
p
affinoid perfectoid space
\text{Spa}(R,R^{+})
is
\mathcal{G}(W(R))
, and
\text{Gr}^{W}_{G,\leq V}

is the analytification of the Witt vector Schubert variety.

Similar to Hecke operators on
\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,\eta}
, we also have them on
\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}
. There is the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},\nu,W}_{0|0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hecke}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
.
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{0}^{(\{1\})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{0}^{(\{1\})}}

Lemma 6.1
.

p_{1}
and
p_{2}
are finite étale.

Proof.

Given a morphism
\text{Spa}(R,R^{+})\rightarrow\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},W}_{0}
from an affinoid characteristic
p
perfectoid space into
\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}
, which is determined by the data

\varphi_{2}:\mathcal{P}_{2}\cong\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{2}.

Its fiber product with
p_{2}
is the (analytification of) refined Witt vector affine Deligne-Lusztig variety
X^{W}_{0,\nu}(1)

6
6

6

See
[
XZ17
, section 4.1.6]
, where the refined affine Deligne-Lusztig variety
X_{\mu,\nu}(b)
is defined as the intersection of the usual affine Deligne-Lusztig variety
X_{\mu}(b)
with the Schubert cells
\operatorname{Gr}_{\nu}
, i.e.
X_{\mu,\nu}(b)=X_{\mu}(b)\cap\operatorname{Gr}_{\nu}
.
(base change to
R
), which parametrizes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{2}}

with
\gamma
bounded by
\nu
(the second line is fixed). This can be also characterized as the fiber product

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{W}_{0,\nu}(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gr_{\nu}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gr_{\nu}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gr_{\nu}^{W}\times Gr_{\nu}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{id}\times\varphi_{2}^{-1}\operatorname{Frob}}

where
a
sends the above diagram to
\gamma\varphi_{1}^{-1}
,
b
maps it to
\gamma

and
\varphi_{2}^{-1}\operatorname{Frob}
is the map sending
\gamma:\mathcal{P}_{1}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{2}
to
\varphi_{2}^{-1}\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\gamma

(fixing a trivialization of
\mathcal{P}_{2}
), as
\varphi_{2}
is an isomorphism, we see that
X^{W}_{0,\nu}(1)\cong Gr_{\nu}^{W}(\mathbb{F}_{p})

as zero dimensional varieties, which is obviously finite étale. Thus
p_{2}
is finite étale, and similarly for
p_{1}
.
∎

Moreover, we see that

\operatorname{Sht}_{0|0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}=L^{+,W}\mathcal{G}\setminus X^{W}_{0,\nu}(1)\cong L^{+,W}\mathcal{G}\setminus Gr_{\nu}^{W}(\mathbb{F}_{p})

which can be identified with the substack

\Gamma_{\nu}^{\operatorname{loc},W}:=\operatorname{Hecke}_{\nu}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\subset\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\setminus G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

defined by the bounded by
\nu
condition. Moreover, we have
\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}=L^{+,W}\mathcal{G}\setminus\bullet
and
\mathscr{S}_{1}^{(\{1\})}=\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
, so any function
f\in C(\Gamma_{\nu}^{W},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
defines a cohomological correspondence
\mathscr{C}_{f}^{\operatorname{loc},W}
from

(\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W},\epsilon_{0}^{(\{1\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})})

to itself supported on
\Gamma_{\nu}^{\operatorname{loc},W}

which is multiplication by
f(x)
at
x\in\Gamma_{\nu}^{\operatorname{loc},W}
. See
[
XZ17
, Proposition 5.4.4]
for more details.

We can do the same construction with truncated moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas as in
[
XZ17
]
to obtain
\mathscr{C}_{f}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}
, and we have

\mathscr{C}_{f}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond\diamond}=\mathscr{C}_{f}^{\operatorname{loc},W}

under the identification between sheaves on truncated and untruncated moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas in appendix
A.3
. Note that this identification only holds at the sheaf level, and not at the space level.

We have
\Gamma_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}=\cup\Gamma_{\nu}^{\operatorname{loc},W}
where
\nu
ranges over highest weights of irreducible summands of
V
, and we can similarly define
\mathscr{C}_{f}^{\operatorname{loc},W}
for
f\in C(\Gamma_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})

and

T_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}:=\mathscr{C}_{h_{V}}^{\operatorname{loc},W}

as cohomological correspondence from

(\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W},\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})})

to itself supported on
\Gamma_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}
,
where
h_{V}
is the function corresponding to
V
under classical Satake isomorphism. Similarly, we can define
T_{V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}=\mathscr{C}_{h_{V}}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}
, and we have

T_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}=T_{V}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond\diamond}

under the canonical identification in
A.3
between sheaves on truncated and untruncated moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas.

Theorem 6.2
.

(Xiao–Zhu)
We have an equality

T_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}=S_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},0,W}

as cohomological correspondence from

(\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W},\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})})

to itself supported on
\Gamma_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}
.

Proof.

This is the analytification of
[
XZ17
, Theorem 6.0.1 (2)]
. More precisely, Xiao–Zhu prove the equality as cohomological correspondences on perfect stacks, and we take the
\diamond\diamond
-analytification of the corresponding cohomological correspondences to get an equality of cohomological correspondences on v-stacks. The comparison results (between cohomological correspondences on perfect stacks and their
\diamond\diamond
-analytification) we proved in section
5
, recalled in the previous paragraph, tells us that the cohomological correspondences obtained by analytification coincide with the ones in the statement of the theorem.
∎

We can now prove the desired
S=T
theorem for moduli spaces of
p
-adic shtukas.

Theorem 6.3
.

There is a canonical identification

S_{V,\eta}=T_{V}

as cohomological correspondence from

(\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,\eta},\epsilon_{\mu}^{(\{1\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{U}^{(\{1\})})

to itself supported on
\Gamma_{V}
.

Remark 6.4
.

Again, there is no difficulty generalizing the theorem to shtukas with several legs (in characteristic zero).

Proof.

Theorem
5.13
and
6.2
together imply that
S_{V,\eta}
is the pullback of
T_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\boxtimes\text{id}
along

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\text{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\text{id}}

which is the same as
T_{V}
. Indeed, we have a commutative diagram with the upper two squares Cartesian

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\nu}_{\mu|\mu,\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},\nu,W}_{0|0}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\text{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\text{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\text{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\text{id}}

where we abuse the notation by writing
\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}
the unique map from
\operatorname{Sht}_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},W}
to the final point
*
. Then under the canonical identification
*\times\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}=\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu,\eta}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}
, the composition is precisely (
21
). Let
\mathscr{C}_{\nu}^{\operatorname{loc},W}=\mathscr{C}_{1_{\Gamma_{\nu}^{W}}}^{\operatorname{loc},W}
with

1_{\Gamma_{\nu}^{\operatorname{loc},W}}
the characteristic function of
\Gamma_{\nu}^{\operatorname{loc},W}
.
Then the pullback of
\mathscr{C}_{\nu}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\boxtimes\text{id}
is

p_{1}^{*}\epsilon_{\mu}^{(\{1\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{U}^{(\{1\})}\cong\pi^{*}(p_{1}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\text{id})^{*}(\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\text{id})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}

\cong\pi^{*}(p_{2}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\text{id})^{*}(\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\text{id})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}

\cong\pi^{*}(p_{2}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\text{id})^{!}(\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\text{id})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}

\overset{\text{BC}}{\longrightarrow}p_{2}^{!}\pi_{1}^{*}(\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\text{id})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\cong p_{2}^{!}\epsilon_{\mu}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\}),*}\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}.

Using that
p_{2}
and
p_{2}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\text{id}
are étale, we see that

\pi^{*}(p_{2}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\text{id})^{*}(\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\text{id})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\cong\pi^{*}(p_{2}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\text{id})^{!}(\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\text{id})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}

\overset{\text{BC}}{\longrightarrow}p_{2}^{!}\pi_{1}^{*}(\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\text{id})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}

is nothing but

\pi^{*}(p_{2}^{\operatorname{loc}}\times\text{id})^{*}(\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\text{id})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\cong p_{2}^{*}\pi_{1}^{*}(\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\text{id})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}

\cong p_{2}^{!}\pi_{1}^{*}(\epsilon_{0}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\times\text{id})^{*}\mathscr{S}_{1}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})}\boxtimes\mathscr{S}_{U}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\})},

thus the pullback of

\mathscr{C}_{\nu}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\boxtimes\text{id}

is simply
\mathscr{C}_{\nu}
, hence the pullback of
T_{V}^{\operatorname{loc},W}\boxtimes\text{id}
is
T_{V}
by adding them together.
∎

7.
S=T
for Shimura varieties

In this section, we assume that
G/\mathbb{Q}
is a reductive group equipped with a
G_{\mathbb{R}}
-conjugacy class
X
of homomorphisms

h:\text{Res}_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow G_{\mathbb{R}}

that gives rise to a Hodge type Shimura variety. Namely, there is a closed embedding
G\hookrightarrow\text{GSp}(Q,\psi)
such that
X
is mapped to the canonical Siegel conjugacy class, where
Q
is a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space,
\psi
is a non-degenerate alternating form on
Q
, and
\text{GSp}(Q,\psi)
is the similitude sympletic group with respect to
(Q,\psi)
, i.e. the automorphism of
Q
preserving
\psi
up to a constant. We fix such an embedding throughout, then by
[
Del82
]
, there is a set of tensors

(s_{\alpha})
in

Q^{\otimes}:=\underset{s,t\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigoplus}Q^{\otimes s}\otimes(Q^{\vee})^{\otimes t}

such that
G
is the stabilizer of
(s_{\alpha})
, which we also fix from now on.

We can base change
h
to
\mathbb{C}
to obtain
h_{\mathbb{C}}:\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow G_{\mathbb{C}}
, where the two factors
\mathbb{G}_{m}
correspond to the identity embedding and the conjugation embedding respectively. Let
\mu:\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow G_{\mathbb{C}}
be the restriction of
h_{\mathbb{C}}
to the first factor, whose conjugacy class has field of definition
E
, which is a number field.

Moreover, we fix a prime
p
such that
G
is hyperspecial at
p
, i.e. there is a reductive group
\mathcal{G}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
with generic fiber
G
. Let
K=K^{p}K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be a compact open subgroup where
K_{p}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})
are compact open. We assume that
K^{p}
is neat for convenience, so that the corresponding Shimura variety is a scheme instead of a stack. By enlarging
Q
, we may assume that
Q
has a lattice
Q_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
on which
\psi
induces a perfect pairing

Q_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\times Q_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
. Moreover, we can assume that the embedding

G\hookrightarrow\text{GSp}(Q,\psi)

induces an embedding

\mathcal{G}\hookrightarrow\text{GSp}(Q_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}},\psi)

as reductive groups over
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
, and the tensors
s_{\alpha}
’s extend to tensors of
Q_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
whose stabilizer group is
\mathcal{G}
, see
[
Kis10
, Section 2.3.2]
for details.

By
[
Kis10
, Lemma 2.1.2]
, there is a compact open subgroup
\tilde{K}^{p}\subset\text{GSp}(Q,\psi)(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that

K^{p}=\tilde{K}^{p}\cap G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and the injection of
G
into
\text{GSp}(Q,\psi)
induces a closed embedding

S_{K}\hookrightarrow\tilde{S}_{\tilde{K}^{p}\tilde{K}_{p}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}E

of Shimura varieties, where

\tilde{K}_{p}:=\text{GSp}(Q_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}},\psi)(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
,

S_{K}
is the canonical model over
E
of the Shimura variety associated to
(G,X)
with level
K
, and
\tilde{S}_{\tilde{K}^{p}\tilde{K}_{p}}

is the Siegel variety associated to
\text{GSp}(Q,\psi)
with level
\tilde{K}^{p}\tilde{K}_{p}
(over
\mathbb{Q}
).

Let us now fix a prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
E
lying above
p
.
By
[
Kis10
]
,
S_{K}
has a canonical smooth integral model
\mathfrak{S}_{K}
over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. It is defined as the normalization of the closure of
S_{K}
in
\mathfrak{A}_{\tilde{K}^{p}\tilde{K}_{p}}
, where

\mathfrak{A}_{\tilde{K}^{p}\tilde{K}_{p}}
is the canonical integral model of
\tilde{S}_{\tilde{K}^{p}\tilde{K}_{p}}

as moduli space of polarized abelian varieties (over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
). By construction, there is a canonical abelian scheme
A
over
\mathfrak{S}_{K}
, being the pullback of the universal one on
\mathfrak{A}_{\tilde{K}^{p}\tilde{K}_{p}}
. We denote
\mathfrak{X}_{K}
the
p
-adic completion of
\mathfrak{S}_{K}
, and
\mathcal{X}_{K}
the adic generic fiber of
\mathfrak{X}_{K}
, i.e. the good reduction locus in
(S_{K}\times_{E}\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{\text{ad}}
. Moreover, we denote

\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K}:=\mathfrak{S}_{K,\overline{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}

the special fiber of
\mathfrak{S}_{K}
.

Remark 7.1
.

The integral model
\mathfrak{S}_{K}
is really defined over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}
, where
v
is a place of
E
lying over
p
, and similarly for the other spaces in the previous paragraph. We can upgrade everything below to this situation.

By construction, the representation
Q
defines a Betti local system

\mathcal{V}_{B}:=Q\times^{G(\mathbb{Q})}(X\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K)

on

S_{K}(\mathbb{C})\cong G(\mathbb{Q})\setminus(X\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K)

which can be identified with the first homology of
A
over
S_{K}(\mathbb{C})
, i.e.
\mathcal{V}_{B}=(R^{1}h_{B,*}\mathbb{Q})^{\vee}

where
h:A\rightarrow S_{K}
is the structure map of
A
, and
h_{B,*}
means that we take pushforward in the classical topology.

We can similarly define

\mathcal{V}_{B}^{\otimes}:=\underset{s,t\in\mathbb{N}}{\bigoplus}\mathcal{V}_{B}^{\otimes s}\otimes(\mathcal{V}_{B}^{\vee})^{\otimes t}

with duals and tensor products in the category of Betti
\mathbb{Q}
-local systems over
S_{K}
. Similar notation will apply to other categories of local systems, namely étale, de Rham (filtered vector bundles with flat connections) and crystalline (
F
-cyrstals).
Now the tensors
s_{\alpha}
’s define global sections

s_{\alpha,B}:1\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{B}^{\otimes}

of
\mathcal{V}_{B}^{\otimes}
since they are
G(\mathbb{Q})
-invariant, where
1
denotes the unit object in the rigid Tannakian category, i.e. the 1 dimensional constant local system.

Let
\ell
be a prime that can be either equal or not equal to
p
.
By
[
Kis10
, Lemma 2.2.1]
,
s_{\alpha,B}
’s determine global sections

s_{\alpha,\ell}:1\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{\ell}^{\otimes}

with

\mathcal{V}_{\ell}:=(R^{1}h_{\text{ét},*}\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})^{\vee}
,
characterized by
s_{\alpha,\ell}
and
s_{\alpha,B}
matching under the comparison isomorphism between Betti and étale cohomology, where
h_{\text{ét},*}
denotes pushforward in the étale topology of
h:A\rightarrow S_{K}
(over
E
). Note that the real content of the statement is that the sections defined by the comparison isomorphism descend to
E
.

We also have the de Rham tensors
