s_{\alpha,\text{dR}}:1\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{\text{dR}}^{\otimes}

where

\mathcal{V}_{\text{dR}}
:=
(R^{1}h_{*}\Omega^{*}_{A/\mathfrak{S}_{K}})^{\vee}

is the relative de Rham homology, which is a filtered vector bundle with flat connection. Over
\mathbb{C}
, these are defined by the comparison between the de Rham and Betti cohomology, which descend to
E
by Deligne’s theory of absolute Hodge and the equivalence of analytic vector bundles with a flat connection and algebraic vector bundles with a flat connection with regular singularities, see
[
Kis10
, Corollary 2.2.2]
for details. Moreover, it is proved in
loc.cit.
that
s_{\alpha,\text{dR}}
’s extend to the integral model.

We can restrict (the analytification of)
\mathcal{V}_{p}
to the good reduction locus
\mathcal{X}_{K}
, then by
[
Sch13
, Theorem 1.10]
, it is a de Rham local system in the sense of
[
Sch13
]
with the associated filtered vector bundle with flat connection
\mathcal{V}_{\text{dR}}
(restricted on
\mathcal{X}_{K}
), i.e. there is an isomorphism

(41)

\mathcal{V}_{p}\otimes_{\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\text{dR}}\cong\mathcal{V}_{\text{dR}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{K}}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\text{dR}},

where
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
is the sheaf on
the proétale site
\mathbb{X}_{\text{proét}}

associated to the topological ring
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and

\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\text{dR}}

is the period sheaf as defined in
[
Sch13
, Definition 6.8]
and corrected in
[
Sch16
]
. See
[
Sch13
, Section 6]
for the definition of other period sheaves used in the following. We also have similar comparison isomorphism between
\mathcal{V}_{p}^{\otimes}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\text{dR}}^{\otimes}
. By the result of Blasius and Wintenberger (
[
Bla94
]
), the isomorphism takes
s_{\alpha,p}\otimes 1

to

s_{\alpha,\text{dR}}\otimes 1
at points defined over number fields, so they are matched globally by the flatness of these sections.

We have two
\mathbb{B}_{\text{dR}}^{+}
-local systems

\mathbb{M}:=\mathcal{V}_{p}\otimes_{\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\text{dR}}^{+}

and

\mathbb{M}_{0}:=(\mathcal{V}_{\text{dR}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{K}}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\text{dR}}^{+})^{\nabla=0}

such that

(42)

\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\text{dR}}^{+}}\mathbb{B}_{\text{dR}}\cong\mathbb{M}_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\text{dR}}^{+}}\mathbb{B}_{\text{dR}},

which is taking
\nabla=0
on both sides of (
41
). Moreover, we
have

\mathbb{M}\subset\mathbb{M}_{0}
, see
[
Sch13
, Proposition 7.9]
. Note that we have used homology instead of cohomology, which corresponds to a reverse of the inclusion. Since the identification (
41
) matches
s_{\alpha,p}\otimes 1
with
s_{\alpha,\text{dR}}\otimes 1
, we see that the same holds for the identification (
42
).

Moreover, by
[
CS17
, Corollary 2.2.4]
, we can take the 0th graded piece of (
41
) to obtain the Hodge–Tate sequence

0\longrightarrow(\text{Lie}\ A)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{K}}}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{K}}(1)\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{K}}\longrightarrow(\text{Lie}\ A^{\vee})^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{K}}}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{K}}\longrightarrow 0,

where

\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{X}_{K}}

is the
p
-adic completion of
\nu^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{K,\text{ét}}}

with
\nu:\mathcal{X}_{K,\text{proét}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{K,\text{ét}}

the structure morphism from the proétale site to the étale site.

Let
D(A)
be the covariant
F
-crystal associated to
A
on the special fiber
\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K}
.
As explained in
[
XZ17
, Section 7.1.5]
, the de Rham tensors give rise to

s_{\alpha,0}:1\longrightarrow D(A)^{\otimes}.

Indeed, as
\mathfrak{S}_{K}
is smooth over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
(and
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
is unramified), the crystal
D(A)
is equivalent to the vector bundle with flat connections
\mathcal{V}_{\operatorname{dR}}
, so
s_{\alpha,\operatorname{dR}}
’s give rise to
s_{\alpha,0}
’s as arrows of crystals. Then it is proved in
[
Kis10
]
that pointwisely
s_{\alpha,0}
’s are arrows of F-crystals, which gives what we want.

We now want to construct a map

\operatorname{loc}_{p}:\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\diamond}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu}

over
\operatorname{Spd}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Recall that
\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\diamond}
is the sheafification of the presheaf sending an affinoid perfectoid
S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
over
\overline{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
to the set of untilts
S^{\sharp}=\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp},R^{\sharp,+})
over
\operatorname{Spa}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
together with a map
\operatorname{Spec}(R^{\sharp,+})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{K}
of schemes over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.

Given

f:\operatorname{Spec}(R^{\sharp,+})\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{K}
,
we have the
p
-divisible group

f^{*}A[p^{\infty}]

over
\operatorname{Spec}(R^{\sharp,+})
,
which by
[
SW20
, Theorem 17.5.2]
is equivalent to a finite projective
A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+}):=W(R^{+})
-module
\mathcal{M}
together with an isomorphism

\varphi_{\mathcal{M}}:\varphi^{*}\mathcal{M}[\frac{1}{\varphi(\xi)}]\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{M}[\frac{1}{\varphi(\xi)}]

such that
\mathcal{M}\subset\varphi_{\mathcal{M}}(\varphi^{*}\mathcal{M})\subset\frac{1}{\varphi(\xi)}\mathcal{M}
,
where
\xi\in A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})

is a generator of the kernel of
A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})\rightarrow R^{\sharp,+}
, and
\varphi
is the canonical lift of Frobenius on
A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})
. It gives rise to a minuscule
GL_{n}
-shtuka with one leg over
\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp},R^{\sharp,+})
by restricting on
\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(R,R^{+})
(and twist by Frobenius), where
n
is twice the relative dimension of
A
over
\mathfrak{S}_{K}
.

Let us first recall how
\mathcal{M}
is constructed in
[
SW20
, Theorem 17.5.2]
. Let
G
be a
p
-divisible group over an integral perfectoid ring
\mathcal{S}
(in the sense of
[
SW20
, Definition 17.5.1]
). If
p=0
in
\mathcal{S}
, then
\mathcal{M}(G)
is given by Dieudonné theory. If
p\neq 0
in
\mathcal{S}
, then Dieudonné theory applied to the restriction of
G
to
\mathcal{S}/p
gives us an
A_{\operatorname{crys}}(\mathcal{S})
-module
\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(G)
together with an isomorphism

\varphi^{*}\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(G)[\frac{1}{p}]\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(G)[\frac{1}{p}],

where
A_{\operatorname{crys}}(\mathcal{S})

is the universal
p
-adically complete divided power thickening of
\mathcal{S}/p
. Now
\mathcal{M}(G)\subset\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(G)
is characterized by being the largest submodule of
\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(G)
such that for every map
\mathcal{S}\rightarrow V
with
V
an integral perfectoid valuation ring with algebraically closed fraction field, its image in
\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(G_{V})

lies in
\mathcal{M}(G_{V})
, where
\mathcal{M}(G_{V})
is the Dieudonné module if
V
is of characteristic
p
. If
p\neq 0
in
V
and
V=\mathcal{O}_{C}
with
C
the fraction field of
V
,
\mathcal{M}(G_{V})
is constructed as in
[
SW20
, Theorem 14.4.1]
. For general
V
not of characteristic
p
, let
k
be the residue field of
\mathcal{O}_{C}
with
C
the fraction field of
V
, and
\bar{V}\subset k
the image of
V
in
k
, then
\mathcal{M}(G_{V})
is defined as
\mathcal{M}(G_{\mathcal{O}_{C}})\times_{\mathcal{M}({G_{k}})}\mathcal{M}(G_{\bar{V}})
, in other words,
\mathcal{M}(G_{V})
is the largest submodule of
\mathcal{M}(G_{\mathcal{O}_{C}})

whose image in
\mathcal{M}(G_{k})
lies in
\mathcal{M}(G_{\bar{V}})
.

In summary, the construction of
\mathcal{M}(G)
is to use v-descent to reduce to Dieudonné theory and the classification over
\mathcal{O}_{C}
. The key input is then the classification in terms of Hodge–Tate periods as in
[
SW13
]
, and Fargues’ theorem (
[
SW20
, Theorem 14.1.1]
) that establishes the equivalence between de Rham pairs and Breuil–Kisin–Fargues modules.

We now prove that the
s_{\alpha}
’s give rise to tensors on
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
R^{\sharp,+}
be an integral perfectoid ring over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
,
f:\operatorname{Spec}(R^{\sharp,+})\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{K}
a map over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, and
G:=f^{*}A[p^{\infty}]
the induced
p
-divisible group over
R^{\sharp,+}
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be the
\varphi
-
A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})
-module corresponding to
G
, then there exists

s_{\alpha,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}:1\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}

such that under the identification (see
[
SW20
, Theorem 17.5.2]
)

\mathcal{M}\otimes_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})}A_{\operatorname{crys}}(R^{\sharp,+})\cong\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}

s_{\alpha,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\otimes 1
is mapped to
s_{\alpha,0}
(restricted from
\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K}
to
R^{\sharp,+}/p
),
where
\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}
is the Dieudonné crystal of
G
restricted to
R^{\sharp,+}/p
. Moreover, when
R^{\sharp,+}=\mathcal{O}_{C}
, then under the identification

\mathcal{M}\otimes_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})}A_{\operatorname{inf}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})[\frac{1}{\mu}]\cong T_{p}G\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A_{\operatorname{inf}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})[\frac{1}{\mu}]

from (the dual of)
[
SW20
, Theorem 14.9.1 (2)]
,

s_{\alpha,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\otimes 1
is sent to
s_{\alpha,p}
.

Proof.

We have

s_{\alpha,0}:1\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}^{\otimes}

by pulling back

s_{\alpha,0}:1\longrightarrow D(A)^{\otimes}

along

\operatorname{Spec}(R^{\sharp,+}/p)\longrightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K}
, the restriction of
f
to the characteristic
p
fibers, and
we are aiming to show that

s_{\alpha,0}

factorizes through
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
. By construction of
\mathcal{M}
that we have recalled above, we need to show that for every map
R^{\sharp,+}\rightarrow V
with V an integral perfectoid valuation ring with algebraically closed fraction field,

s_{\alpha,0}|_{V}:1\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(G_{V})^{\otimes}

factorizes through
\mathcal{M}(G_{V})^{\otimes}
. If
V
has characteristic
p
, then

W(V)
is a (pro-)PD-thickening and
\mathcal{M}(G_{V})=D(A_{V})(W(V))
. We can evaluate the crystal
D(A_{V})
at
W(V)

to obtain the desired factorization.

If
V
has mixed characteristic and
V=\mathcal{O}_{C}
with
C
the fraction field of
V
, then
\mathcal{M}(G_{V})
is the Breuil–Kisin–Fargues module corresponding to the Hodge–Tate periods of
G_{V}
. Let us recall its construction first. We have the Hodge–Tate exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\operatorname{Lie}\ G_{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}C(1)\longrightarrow T_{p}G_{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}C\longrightarrow(\operatorname{Lie}\ G_{V}^{*})^{*}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}C\longrightarrow 0,

which gives rise to a
\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C)
-submodule

\Xi\subset T_{p}G_{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C)

characterized by

\Xi\ \operatorname{mod}\ t\subset T_{p}G_{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}C

being

\operatorname{Lie}\ G_{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}C(1)\subset T_{p}G_{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}C
, where
t\in\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C)
is a generator of the kernel
\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C)\rightarrow C
. We have now a de Rham pair
(\Xi,T_{p}G_{V})
, which by
[
SW20
, Theorem 14.1.1]
is equivalent to a minuscule Breuil–Kisin–Fargues module
\mathcal{M}(G_{V})
. Moreover, we have by
[
SW20
, Proposition 14.8.1]
that

\Xi\subset T_{p}G_{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}(C)

is the same as

\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(G_{V})\otimes_{A_{\operatorname{crys}}(V)}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C)\subset T_{p}G_{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}(C)

under a canonical identification

\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(G_{V})\otimes_{\operatorname{A}_{\operatorname{crys}}(V)}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}(C)\cong T_{p}G_{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}(C),

where the identification actually holds over a smaller period ring
\operatorname{B}_{\operatorname{crys}}
.

Now as
G_{V}
is the pullback of
A[p^{\infty}]
along a map
\operatorname{Spec}(V)\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{K}
, we have

s_{\alpha,p}:1\longrightarrow(T_{p}G_{V})^{\otimes}

which by the following lemma matches with

s_{\alpha,0}:1\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(G_{V})^{\otimes}

under the identification

\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(G_{V})\otimes_{\operatorname{A}_{\operatorname{crys}}(V)}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}(C)\cong T_{p}G_{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}(C).

It follows that
s_{\alpha,0}
and
s_{\alpha,p}
glue to an arrow of de Rham pairs

s_{\alpha,\operatorname{BdR}}:1\longrightarrow(\Xi,T_{p}G_{V})^{\otimes},

which by
[
SW20
, Theorem 14.1.1]
is equivalent to

s_{\alpha,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}:1\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}(G_{V})^{\otimes}.

The relation with
s_{\alpha,0}
is clear from the construction.

Lastly, if
V
is of mixed characteristic but not necessarily equals to
\mathcal{O}_{C}
, then
V
is the pullback of
\bar{V}\subset k
along
\mathcal{O}_{C}\rightarrow k
, where
k
is the residue field of
\mathcal{O}_{C}
and
\bar{V}
is the image of
V
in
k
. We have a similar description of
\mathcal{M}(G_{V})
, being

\mathcal{M}(G_{\mathcal{O}_{C}})\times_{\mathcal{M}({G_{k}})}\mathcal{M}(G_{\bar{V}})
. We have already produced
s_{\alpha,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
for
\mathcal{M}(G_{\mathcal{O}_{C}})
,
\mathcal{M}(G_{\bar{V}})
and
\mathcal{M}(G_{k})
individually, it remains to see that they glue to
\mathcal{M}(G_{V})
. This is clear as they are pullback from the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(\bar{V})}

and we have the identification

\mathcal{M}(G_{\mathcal{O}_{C}})\otimes_{\operatorname{A}_{\operatorname{inf}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})}W(k)\cong(\mathcal{M}(G_{\mathcal{O}_{C}})\otimes_{\operatorname{A}_{\operatorname{inf}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})}\operatorname{A}_{\operatorname{crys}}(\mathcal{O}_{C}))\otimes_{\operatorname{A}_{\operatorname{crys}}}W(k)

\cong\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(G_{\mathcal{O}_{C}/p})\otimes_{\operatorname{A}_{\operatorname{crys}}}W(k)\cong\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(G_{k}).

∎

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
f:\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{C})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{K}
be a map over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, and
H_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}
be the pullback of
A[p^{\infty}]
along
f
, then (pullback of)
s_{\alpha,0}\otimes 1
is mapped to
s_{\alpha,p}\otimes 1

under the canonical isomorphism

\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(H_{V})\otimes_{A_{\operatorname{crys}}(V)}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}(C)\cong T_{p}H_{V}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}(C).

Proof.

Using the canonical duality between crystalline cohomology (resp. étale cohomology) of abelian varieties and Dieudonné modules (resp. Tate modules) of the associated
p
-divisible groups (see
[
SW20
, Proposition 14.9.3]
), it is enough to prove the corresponding statements for abelian varieties, namely
(s_{\alpha,p})^{\vee}\otimes 1
is mapped to
(s_{\alpha,0})^{\vee}\otimes 1
under the étale-crystalline comparison

H^{1}_{\text{ét}}(A_{C},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}(C)\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{crys}}(A_{\mathcal{O}_{C}/p}/A_{\operatorname{crys}}(\mathcal{O}_{C}))\otimes_{A_{\operatorname{crys}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}(C).

Since
\mathcal{O}_{C}
is local,
f
factorizes through an affine open
\operatorname{Spec}(T)
of
\mathfrak{S}_{K}
, which is smooth over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. By smoothness, we can lift
f
to

g:\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\operatorname{crys}}(\mathcal{O}_{C}))\longrightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(T),

then

(43)

H^{1}_{\operatorname{crys}}(A_{\mathcal{O}_{C}/p}/A_{\operatorname{crys}}(\mathcal{O}_{C}))\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(g^{*}A)\cong g^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{\operatorname{dR}})^{\vee}

where
A_{\mathcal{O}_{C}/p}
is the restriction of
f^{*}A
to
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{C}/p)
.

Moreover, let

h:\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C))\longrightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(T)

be the composition of
g
with the canonical map

r:\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C))\longrightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\operatorname{crys}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})),

which is a lift of (restriction of)
f
. It follows from the proof of
[
BMS18
, Theorem 13.8]
that

(44)

H^{1}_{\operatorname{crys}}(A_{C}/\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C))\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(h^{*}A)\cong h^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{\operatorname{dR}})^{\vee}

where
A_{C}:=(f^{*}A)_{C}
, and
H^{1}_{\operatorname{crys}}(A_{C}/\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C))

is defined as in
[
BMS18
, Theorem 13.1]
.

Now under the identification (
44
) and (
43
), the canonical isomorphism

(45)

H^{1}_{\operatorname{crys}}(A_{C}/\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C))\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{crys}}(A_{\mathcal{O}_{C}/p}/A_{\operatorname{crys}}(\mathcal{O}_{C}))\otimes_{A_{\operatorname{crys}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C)

from
[
BMS18
, Lemma 13.11]
is nothing but

r^{*}g^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{\operatorname{dR}})^{\vee}\cong h^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{\operatorname{dR}})^{\vee}.

We have

H^{1}_{\operatorname{crys}}(A_{C}/\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C))\cong f^{*}\mathbb{M}_{0}^{\vee},

under which
f^{*}(s_{\alpha,\operatorname{dR}})^{\vee}
is mapped to
h^{*}(s_{\alpha,\operatorname{dR}})^{\vee}
using the identification (
44
). We now abuse the notation by writing
(s_{\alpha,\operatorname{dR}})^{\vee}
the corresponding tensors on
H^{1}_{\operatorname{crys}}(A_{C}/\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C))
.
Since
(s_{\alpha,0})^{\vee}
is
g^{*}(s_{\alpha,\operatorname{dR}})^{\vee}
,
it follows that the isomorphism (
45
) takes
(s_{\alpha,\operatorname{dR}})^{\vee}
to
(s_{\alpha,0})^{\vee}\otimes 1
.

On the other hand, we have the canonical isomorphism

(46)

H^{1}_{\text{ét}}(A_{C},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}(C)\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{crys}}(A_{C}/\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C))\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}(C)}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}(C)

from
[
BMS18
, Theorem 13.1]
, which is nothing but the pullback of isomorphism (
42
) along
f
. Hence
(s_{\alpha,p})^{\vee}\otimes 1
is mapped to
(s_{\alpha,\operatorname{dR}})^{\vee}\otimes 1
through (
46
). Now the composition of isomorphisms (
46
) and (
45
) is

H^{1}_{\text{ét}}(A_{C},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}(C)\cong H^{1}_{\operatorname{crys}}(A_{\mathcal{O}_{C}/p}/A_{\operatorname{crys}}(\mathcal{O}_{C}))\otimes_{A_{\operatorname{crys}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})}\mathbb{B}_{\operatorname{dR}}(C),

which is exactly the crystalline comparison isomorphism, see
[
SW20
, Proposition 14.7.2]
. It
matches
(s_{\alpha,p})^{\vee}\otimes 1
with
(s_{\alpha,0})^{\vee}\otimes 1
as the two intermediate isomorphisms do.
∎

We now have a
\mathcal{G}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})
-quasitorsor
\mathcal{E}
over
\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+}))
that parametrizes isomorphisms between
Q_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})

and
\mathcal{M}
that preserves the Hodge tensors, i.e. for any
A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})
-algebra
T
,

\mathcal{E}(T)=\{\beta:Q_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}T\cong\mathcal{M}\otimes_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})}T\ \ |\beta(s_{\alpha}\otimes 1)=s_{\alpha,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\otimes 1\}.

Proposition 7.4
.

\mathcal{E}
is a
\mathcal{G}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})
-torsor over
\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+}))
.

Proof.

We write
\mathcal{E}_{T}
as the base change of
\mathcal{E}
to
\operatorname{Spec}(T)
for any
A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})
-algebra
T
.

Let us consider the ringed site
(\mathcal{C}_{R^{+}},\mathcal{O})
consists of perfect
R^{+}
-algebras with v-topology and sheaf of rings
\mathcal{O}(-):=W(-)
. We can view
\mathcal{E}
as a sheaf

7
7

7

It is a sheaf as v-topology is subcanonical on perfect schemes, see
[
BS16
, Remark 4.2]
.

on
(\mathcal{C}_{R^{+}},\mathcal{O})

with value
\mathcal{E}(W(S))
for a perfect
R^{+}
-algebra
S
.

It follows from
[
BS16
, Theorem 4.1 (ii)]
that (finite) locally free sheaves on
(\mathcal{C}_{R^{+}},\mathcal{O})
are the same as vector bundles on
W(R^{+})
, thus
\mathcal{G}
-torsors on
(\mathcal{C}_{R^{+}},\mathcal{O})
(in the sense of
[
Sta22
, Tag 03AH]
) are the same as
\mathcal{G}
-torsors on
W(R^{+})
by the Tannakian formalism. Thus it is enough to prove that
\mathcal{E}
is a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor on
(\mathcal{C}_{R^{+}},\mathcal{O})
, i.e.
\mathcal{E}_{W(S)}

has a section for a v-cover
S
of
R^{+}
. By the equivalence between torsors on
(\mathcal{C}_{S},\mathcal{O})
and
W(S)
, it is enough to prove that
\mathcal{E}_{W(S)}

is a torsor for some v-cover
S
of
R
, which
we can choose to be a (possibly infinite) product of perfect valuation rings with algebraically closed fraction fields, and we quickly reduce to
S=V
a perfect valuation ring with algebraically closed fraction field.

Let
\xi\in A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})
be a generator of the kernel of
A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})\twoheadrightarrow R^{\sharp,+}
, then
\xi\in W(V)
using the map
R\rightarrow V
. Taking the completion if necessary, we can assume that
V
is
\xi\ \operatorname{mod}\ p
-adically complete, so
V^{\sharp}=W(V)/\xi
is integral perfectoid, which is also a valuation ring with algebraically closed fraction field. We have
R^{\sharp,+}\rightarrow V^{\sharp}
, and let
G_{V^{\sharp}}
be the pullback of
A[p^{\infty}]
along

\operatorname{Spec}(V^{\sharp})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(R^{\sharp,+})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{K},

then

\mathcal{E}_{W(V)}=\operatorname{Isom}_{s_{\alpha}}(Q_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}W(V),\mathcal{M}(G_{V^{\sharp}})).

If the characteristic of
V^{\sharp}
is
p
, then
\mathcal{E}_{W(V)}
is the pullback of (the homology version and not mesmerizing trivilization of Tate twist)
\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{crys}}
as defined in
[
XZ17
, Corollary 7.1.14]
, which is proved in
loc.cit.
to be a torsor.

If
V^{\sharp}
is of mixed characteristic, and
V^{\sharp}=\mathcal{O}_{C}
with
C
the fraction field of
V^{\sharp}
, then we have

\mathcal{M}(G_{V^{\sharp}})\otimes_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})}A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})[\frac{1}{\mu}]\cong T_{p}G_{V^{\sharp}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})[\frac{1}{\mu}]

that matches
s_{\alpha,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\otimes 1
with
s_{\alpha,p}\otimes 1
, where
\mu=[\epsilon]-1\in A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})
with
\epsilon=(1,\zeta_{p},\zeta_{p^{2}},\cdots)\in\mathcal{O}_{C}^{\flat}

for a chosen compatible
p
-power roots of unity
\zeta_{p^{\bullet}}
. But we already know that
s_{\alpha,p}
matches with
s_{\alpha,B}
under Betti-étale comparison, from which we easily deduce an isomorphism

T_{p}G_{V^{\sharp}}\cong Q_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}

that matches
s_{\alpha,p}
with
s_{\alpha}\otimes 1
. Thus

\mathcal{E}_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})[\frac{1}{\mu}]}

is a torsor. On the other hand,
we have another canonical isomorphism

\mathcal{M}(G_{V^{\sharp}})\otimes_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})}A_{\operatorname{crys}}(V^{\sharp})\cong\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{crys}}(G_{V^{\sharp}}),

matching
s_{\alpha,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\otimes 1
with
s_{\alpha,0}
,
so
\mathcal{E}_{A_{\operatorname{crys}}(V^{\sharp})}
is a torsor as it is again the pullback of torsor
\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{crys}}
in
[
XZ17
, Corollary 7.1.14]
. We know from the proof of
[
BMS18
, Corollary 7.1.14]
that
A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})\rightarrow A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})[\frac{1}{p}]^{\wedge}_{\mu}
factors through
A_{\operatorname{crys}}(V^{\sharp})
, so
\mathcal{E}_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})[\frac{1}{p}]^{\wedge}_{\mu}}

is a torsor. Now Beauville–Laszlo (see
[
Ans22
, Lemma 7.2]
for example) applied to
A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})[\frac{1}{p}]
tells us that
\mathcal{E}_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})[\frac{1}{p}]}
is a torsor.

We have seen that
\mathcal{E}_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})}

is a torsor over the open subset

U:=\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})[\frac{1}{p}])\bigcup\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})[\frac{1}{\mu}]),

whose complement is the unique closed point of
\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp}))

since
A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})/(p,\mu)\cong\mathcal{O}_{C}^{\flat}/(\epsilon-1)
where
\epsilon-1
is a pseudouniformizer in
\mathcal{O}_{C}^{\flat}
. Now it follows from
[
Ans22
, Proposition 8.5]
and
[
SW20
, Lemma 14.2.3]
that torsors (with respect to a reductive group) on
U
are equivalent to torsors on
\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp}))
. Alternatively, we can use the Tannakian formalism,
[
SW20
, Lemma 14.2.3]
and the proof of
[
Ans22
, Proposition 7.3]
(which shows the exactness of equivalence in
[
SW20
, Lemma 14.2.3]
) to obtain the equivalence between torsors (without the reductive hypothesis) on U and
\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp}))
. It follows that
\mathcal{E}_{U}
is a trivial torsor as

\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp}))

is strictly Henselian.

We can now conclude that

\mathcal{E}_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})}

is a trivial torsor. As
\mathcal{E}_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})}
is by construction a quasi-torsor, it is enough to show that it has a section over
A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})
. We have seen that
\mathcal{E}_{U}
is a trivial torsor, so there is a section

U\overset{s}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{E}_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})}
giving rise to a map of
A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})
-algebras

s^{*}:\Gamma(\mathcal{E}_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})}})\longrightarrow\Gamma(U,\mathcal{O}_{U})=A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp}),

where the last equality follows from the proof of
[
BMS18
, Lemma 4.6]
.
Now we note that
\mathcal{E}

is affine over
\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+}))

since it is a closed subscheme of a trivial
\operatorname{GL}(\mathcal{M})
-torsor, so the map
s^{*}
defines a section

\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp}))\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})}

proving the claim.

Lastly, if
V^{\sharp}
is of mixed characteristic without being equal to
\mathcal{O}_{C}
, then
V^{\sharp}
is the pullback of
\overline{V^{\sharp}}\hookrightarrow k
along
\mathcal{O}_{C}\rightarrow k
, where
k
is the residue field of
\mathcal{O}_{C}
and
\overline{V^{\sharp}}
is the image of
V^{\sharp}
in
k
. Now
\mathcal{E}_{A_{\operatorname{inf}}(V^{\sharp})}

is equivalent to

8
8

8

See the beginning of the proof of
[
SW20
, Theorem 17.5.2]
.

\mathcal{E}_{W(\overline{V^{\sharp}})}
and

\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{A}_{\operatorname{inf}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})}

together with an identification
(\mathcal{E}_{W(\overline{V^{\sharp}})})|_{W(k)}\cong(\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{A}_{\operatorname{inf}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})})|_{W(k)}
,
which is a torsor as we have already seen
\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{A}_{\operatorname{inf}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})}
and
\mathcal{E}_{W(\overline{V^{\sharp}})}

are torsors.
∎

We now come back to our construction of the map

\operatorname{loc}_{p}:\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\diamond}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu}.

Given a characteristic
p
affinoid perfectoid space
S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, and an untilt
S^{\sharp}=\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp},R^{\sharp,+})
together with a map
\operatorname{Spec}(R^{\sharp,+})\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{K}
over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, we have constructed a
\mathcal{G}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})
-torsor
over
\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+}))

parametrizing isomorphisms between
Q_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})

and
\mathcal{M}
preserving Hodge tensors. Now the
\varphi
-module structure on
\mathcal{M}
(and
s_{\alpha,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
are compatible with the
\varphi
-structure) gives us a modification

\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}:\varphi^{*}\mathcal{E}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{E}

at
\varphi(\xi)
, where
\xi\in A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})
is a generator of the kernel
A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})\rightarrow R^{\sharp,+}
. In other words,
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}
is an isomorphism on
A_{\operatorname{inf}}(R^{\sharp,+})[\frac{1}{\varphi(\xi)}]
(meromorphicity at
\xi
is automatic at the algebraic setting). We then obtain a
\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-shtuka by twisting
\mathcal{E}
by
\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{-1}
and restricting to
\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(S)
. More precisely, let

\mathcal{P}:=(\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{-1})^{*}\mathcal{E}|_{\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(S)},

then
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}

gives rise to a modification

\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}:\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}(\mathcal{P})\dashrightarrow\mathcal{P}

at the untilt
S^{\sharp}
, i.e. a shtuka with one leg at
S^{\sharp}
.

Lemma 7.5
.

The modification
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}
is bounded by
\mu
, where
\mu
is, by abuse of notation, a dominant cocharacter in the conjugacy class of
\mu
, the restriction of the Deligne homomorphism
h_{\mathbb{C}}
to the first factor.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{G}
is a subgroup of
\operatorname{GSp}(Q,\psi)
, it is enough to prove that the corresponding modification between
\operatorname{GSp}(Q,\psi)
-torsors is bounded by
\mu
(viewed as the a cocharacter of
\operatorname{GSp}(Q,\psi)
). In other words, we need to prove that the resulting sympletic shtuka is bounded by the dominant cocharacter
(1^{(g)},0^{(g)})
of
\operatorname{Gsp(Q,\psi))}
with
2g=\operatorname{dim}Q
.

Since boundedness is a pointwise condition on
R^{\sharp,+}
, we can assume that
R^{\sharp,+}=\mathcal{O}_{C}
for an algebraically closed field
C
. If
C
is of mixed characteristic, then we know from
[
SW20
, Proposition 12.4.6]
and remark 12.4.7 that the relative position of
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}
is governed by the Hodge–Tate period
\text{Lie}\ G_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}C(1)\subset T_{p}G_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}C
(and the corresponding Hodge tensors), but
\text{Lie}\ G_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}
corresponds to the Hodge filtration on the Betti homology of
A_{C}
, and there are isomorphisms respecting Hodge tensors and filtrations, see
[
CS17
, Lemma 2.3.6]
for example.

If
C
is of equal characteristic, then
\mathcal{M}
is the (covariant) Dieudonné crystal associated to
G_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}
evaluated at the initial PD-thickening
W(\mathcal{O}_{C})
of
\mathcal{O}_{C}
, which is the de Rham homology of
A_{W(\mathcal{O}_{C})}
. We can further reduce to geometric points
k
of
\mathcal{O}_{C}
, then it is well-known that the relative position of the
F
-structure on
\mathcal{M}_{W(k)}
is governed by the Hodge filtration on the de Rham cohomology of
A_{W(k)}
, which again can be compared to the Hodge filtration on Betti-cohomology respecting Hodge tensors.
∎

We have now associated a
\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-shtuka
\mathcal{P}
with one leg at
S^{\sharp}
bounded by
\mu
to each element of
\mathfrak{S}_{K}(R^{\sharp,+})
. This defines a functor from the presheaf on affinoid perfectoid spaces over
\overline{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
, which sends
S
to untilts
S^{\sharp}=\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp},R^{\sharp,+})
together with elements in

\mathfrak{S}_{K}(R^{\sharp,+})
, to the v-stack of moduli spaces of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-shtukas over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. Since
\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\diamond}
is the sheafification of the presheaf, we have the desired

\operatorname{loc}_{p}:\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\diamond}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu}.

Let
p-\operatorname{Isog}
be the presheaf on schemes over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
sending a scheme
T
to the set of triples
(x,y,f)
with two points
x,y\in\mathfrak{S}_{K}(T)
, and
f:A_{x}\rightarrow A_{y}
is a
p
-quasi-isogeny from
A_{x}
to
A_{y}
, which are the pullback of
A
along
x
and
y
. Moreover, we require that at every geometric point
s
,
f
preserves the level structure,
s_{\alpha,\operatorname{dR}}
and
s_{\alpha,l}
with
l\neq p
. Further, it preserves
s_{\alpha,0}
(resp.
s_{\alpha,p}
) if
s
is of characteristic
p
(resp. 0). Then
p-\operatorname{Isog}
is represented by a scheme, and we have a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p-\operatorname{Isog}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

where
s
(resp.
t
) remembers
x
(resp.
y
). Moreover,
s
and
t
are proper surjevtive, and étale on the generic fiber. See
[
Lee20
, Ssection 6]
for example.

Similar to
\operatorname{loc}_{p}
, we can construct a map

\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{\operatorname{H}}:p-\operatorname{Isog}^{\diamond}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu}.

Given
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
affinoid pefectoid of characteristic
p
,
\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp},R^{\sharp,+})
an untilt and
(x,y,f)
a point of
p-\operatorname{Isog}(R^{\sharp,+})
, let
\mathcal{M}_{x}
(resp.

) be the
\varphi
-

-module associated to
A_{x}[p^{\infty}]
(resp.
A_{y}[p^{\infty}]
). Then
f
induces an isomorphism
\mathcal{M}_{x}[\frac{1}{p}]\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{M}_{y}[\frac{1}{p}]
(as
f
is a
p
-quasi-isogeny), which preserves
s_{\alpha,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
by proposition
7.2
and
f
preserving
s_{\alpha,0}
, so it induces a modification

f_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{P}_{x}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{y}

at the characteristic
p
untilt, where
\mathcal{P}_{x}
and
\mathcal{P}_{y}
denotes the image of
x
and
y
under
\operatorname{loc}_{p}
. Now
\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{H}
is defined by (sheafification of) sending the data of
(x,y,f)
to
f_{\mathcal{P}}
.

Let
\nu
be a cocharacter of
G
.
We note that if we restrict to the subscheme
p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu}
parametrizing quasi-isogenies of types bounded by
\nu
, i.e. the corresponding maps between shtukas assoicated to the abelian schemes with Hodge tensors are bounded by
\nu
, then
\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{H}
restricts to

\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{\operatorname{H}}:p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu}^{\diamond}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu}^{\nu}.

Now we have a commutative diagram

By the following lemma, we can pullback cohomological correspondence along the diagram, so we have the cohomological correspondence

(48)

\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{*}S_{V}:(\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\diamond},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})\longrightarrow(\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\diamond},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})

supported on
p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu}^{\diamond}
,
where we have used that
\mu
is minuscule so that the structure sheaf
\mathscr{S}_{U}
on
\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu}
is
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
.

Lemma 7.6
.

The diagram factorizes through

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu}^{\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu}^{\nu}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{H}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\operatorname{loc}_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\operatorname{loc}_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}

where
\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\dagger}

(resp.

p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu}^{\dagger}
)
is the canonical compactification
9
9

9

This is different from the toroidal compactifications or any algebraic compactifications of the scheme.
of

\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\diamond}

(resp.

p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu}^{\diamond}
), and the vertical maps along the first row are the canonical maps into the compactifications, see
[
Sch17
, Proposition 18.6]
for the definition of these compactifications. Moreover, the vertical maps along the first line are open immersions, and
the two lower squares in the diagram are Cartesian, so we can pullback cohomological correspondences (we can pullback along smooth maps).

Proof.

Recall that
\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\dagger}
is the v-sheaf sending
S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
to

\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\diamond}(\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{\circ})),

in other words, it is the sheafification of the presheaf sending
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
to the set of untilts
\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp},R^{\sharp,+})
of
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})

over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

together with morphisms
\operatorname{Spec}(R^{\sharp,\circ})\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{K}

of schemes over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, and similarly for

p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu}^{\dagger}
. The canonical map
\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\diamond}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\dagger}

is simply induced by the composition of the restriction map from
\operatorname{Spec}(R^{\sharp,\circ})\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(R^{\sharp,+})
. Since the v-stacks
\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu}
and
\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu}^{\nu}
are independent of
R^{+}
, the factorization follows. Indeed, it follows from the proof of
[
SW20
, Proposition 11.2.1]
that the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(S)}

on

\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(S)

only depends on
R
(rather than
R^{+}
), so the category of vector bundles on
\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(S)

is independent of
R^{+}
, so does the category of torsors on
\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(S)

by the Tannakian formalism.

It follows from
[
Sch17
, Proposition 27.5]
that
\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\diamond}
and
p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu}^{\diamond}
are compactifiable over
\operatorname{Spd}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, so proposition 22.3 (1) of
loc.cit.
tells us that the natural maps into their canonical compactifications are open immersions, i.e. the vertical maps along the first row are open immersions.

Next, we prove the lower left square is Cartesian, the right one is similar. Let
Y
be the fiber product of
p_{1}
and
\overline{\operatorname{loc}_{p}}
, then the induced map

p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu}^{\dagger}\longrightarrow Y

is proper since
s
is proper (and
Y\longrightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\dagger}

is separated, being the base change of
p_{1}
). It follows from
[
Sch17
, Lemma 12.5]
that to prove

p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu}^{\dagger}\longrightarrow Y

is an isomorphism, it is enough to prove it is an isomorphism on geometric points. Thus we can assume that

S^{\sharp}=\operatorname{Spa}(C,C^{+})

where
C
is an algebraically closed perfectoid field. Since both
\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu}^{\nu}
and
p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu}^{\dagger}

are independent of
R^{+}
, we can further assume that
C^{+}=\mathcal{O}_{C}
.

We know that

p
-quasi-isogenies from an abelian variety
A_{x}
preserving Hodge tensors and level structures are the same as
p
-quasi-isogenies from
A_{x}[p^{\infty}]
preserving Hodge tensors, since
p
-quasi-isogenies do not affect level structures (the level structure at
p
is hyperspecial) and
s_{\alpha,l}
while the rest Hodge tensors are all detected by the corresponding
p
-divisible groups. Moreover, we know from
[
SW20
, Theorem 17.5.2]
that
p
-quasi-isogenies between
p
-divisible groups preserving Hodge tensors are the same as
p
-quasi-isogenies between
\mathcal{M}_{x}
and
\mathcal{M}_{y}
preserving
s_{\alpha,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
, so it remains to show these are the same as the corresponding
p
-quasi-isogenies between
\mathcal{E}_{x}
and
\mathcal{E}_{y}
, whence
\mathcal{P}_{x}
and
\mathcal{P}_{y}
. In other words, we want to know that the restriction functor from Breuil–Kisin–Fargues modules
\mathcal{M}
(over
\mathcal{O}_{C}
) to
GL_{n}
-shtukas (over
\operatorname{Spa}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
) is fully faithful up to quasi-isogeny, but this follows from
[
SW20
, Theorem 13.2.1, 14.2.1]
.
∎

Remark 7.7
.

The reason we use the factorization as in the lemma is because the squares in (
47
) are not Cartesian, and the reason for that is we do not have fully faithfulness of the restriction functor from Breuil–Kisin–Fargues modules over
W(R^{+})
to shtukas over
\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+}))
in general, disproving the expectation in
[
Ked19
, Remark 4.5.13]
. Indeed, Breuil–Kisin–Fargues modules over
W(R^{+})
depend on
R^{+}
while shtukas over
\mathcal{Y}_{[0,\infty)}(\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+}))
do not, and there are examples where the base change of Breuil–Kisin–Fargues modules (even those coming from
p
-divisible groups) from
W(R^{+})
to
W(R^{\circ})
is not fully faithful. This is closely related to the failure of Tate’s theorem on homomorphisms between
p
-divisible groups (the characteristic
p
case is due to de Jong) in the case of non-discrete valuation rings.

Indeed, let
K
be the completion of an algebraic closure of
\mathbb{F}_{p}((T))

with ring of integral elements
R
, and
H=E[p^{\infty}]

be the

p
-divisible group of an elliptic curve
E
over
R
which is ordinary at the generic fiber and supersingular at the special fiber.
Then since the generic fiber is algebraically closed,
H_{K}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times\mu_{p^{\infty}}
. Now let
H^{\prime}=(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times\mu_{p^{\infty}})_{R}

be the constant
p
-divisible group defined over
R
, then the isomorphism
H_{K}\cong H^{\prime}_{K}
cannot be extended to an isomorphism over
R
since they are not isomorphic on the special fiber. The Newton polygons are different as
E
is supersingular at the special fiber. Hence the base change to the generic fiber is not fully faithful

10
10

10

The author would like to thank Kiran Kedlaya and Marco D’Addezio for discussions on this point.
.

As a concrete example, let

E:y^{2}=x(x-1)(x-t)

be defined over
\mathbb{F}_{p}[t][\frac{1}{t(t-1)}]
with
p>2
, and
E
is ordinary over the fraction field by
[
Sil09
, exercise 5.8]
.
Now we pick
\lambda\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
such that
E
is supersingular at
t=\lambda
. For example, we can take
p=11
and
\lambda=2
, see
[
Sil09
, example 4.3]
. Now we base change
E
to the completion of
\mathbb{F}_{p}[t][\frac{1}{t(t-1)}]

at
t-\lambda
, which is isomorphic to
\mathbb{F}_{p}[[T]]
, and we further base change to
R
.

We now restrict to the generic fiber to have

(49)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu,\eta}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{K}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{K}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu,\eta}^{\nu}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,\eta},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{loc}_{p,\eta}^{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{loc}_{p,\eta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{loc}_{p,\eta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}

along which we can pullback the Hecke correspondence

\mathscr{C}_{\nu}
in (
22
)
to obtain

\mathscr{C}_{\nu}^{S}:(\mathcal{X}_{K}^{\diamond},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{X}_{K}^{\diamond},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}),

where we use that
\mu
is minuscule so that the canonical sheaves are constant. Note that we can pullback cohomological correspondences here by lemma
7.6
, which shows that it is a composition of open immersion and Cartesian squares, and we can pullback cohomological correspondences along either Cartesian squares or smooth maps. Moreover, it follows from the étaleness of the two correspondences that
\mathscr{C}_{\nu}^{S}
is nothing but

\mathscr{C}_{\nu}^{S}:s^{*}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}\cong\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}\cong t^{*}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}\cong t^{!}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}.

Now as in section
4
, let
V
be a representation of
\hat{G}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, and
h_{V}
be the function corresponding to
V
through classical Satake,
we define

\Gamma_{V}^{S}:=\underset{h_{V}(\nu(p))\neq 0}{\cup}p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu,\eta},

and

T_{V}^{S}:=\underset{h_{V}(\nu(p))\neq 0}{\sum}h_{V}(\nu(p))\mathscr{C}_{\nu}^{S}

which is a cohomological correspondence from
(\mathcal{X}_{K}^{\diamond},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
to itself supported on
\Gamma_{V}^{S,\diamond}
. Note that
T_{V}^{S}
is exactly the (analytification of) Hecke correspondence of
\mathcal{X}_{K}
, so we have proved

Theorem 7.8
.

The pullback of
S_{V,\eta}
along
(
49
) is exactly
T_{V}^{S}
.

Proof.

This follows from the above discussion and theorem
6.3
.
∎

Next we want to compare the Hecke operator and excursion operator on the compactly supported cohomology of
\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K}
, and deduce the
S=T
conjecture of Xiao–Zhu. We begin with a well-known observation.

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
\pi:\mathfrak{S}_{K}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
be the structure map of
\mathfrak{S}_{K}
, then
R^{i}\pi_{!}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
is a lisse sheaf on
\operatorname{Spec}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
for every
i
.

Proof.

It follows from the existence of integral toroidal compactifications (
[
Per19
]
) that
R^{j}\pi_{*}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
is lisse (using the Leray spectral sequence of the open immersion into toroidal compactification and purity of normal crossing divisors). Now Poincare duality gives the result for
R^{i}\pi_{!}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
.
∎

Lemma 7.10
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond})_{!}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}\cong c_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}^{*}R^{i}\pi_{!}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell},

with notations as in appendix
A.1
.

Proof.

It is enough to prove the statement with
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
replaced by
\Lambda
, a finite extension of
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
. It is enough to prove the identification on the special fiber and generic fiber. Over the special fiber, this is proposition
A.3
.

Let
\bar{\eta}
be a completed algebraically closure of
\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, then we have

(R(\pi^{\diamond})_{!}\Lambda)_{\bar{\eta}}\cong R\Gamma_{c}((\mathfrak{S}_{K}^{\diamond})_{\bar{\eta}},\Lambda)\cong R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{K,\bar{\eta}},\Lambda)\cong R\Gamma_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},R\psi(\Lambda))\cong R\Gamma_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},\Lambda)

where
R\psi
is the nearby cycle functor with respect to the integral model
\mathfrak{S}_{K}/\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.
The first isomorphism is proper base change (
[
Sch17
, Proposition 22.15]
), the second is
[
Sch17
, Lemma 15.6]
, the third is
[
Hub13
, Theorem 5.7.8]
, and the last follows from
\mathfrak{S}_{K}
being smooth over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.

On the other hand, we have

(R\pi_{!}\Lambda)_{\bar{\eta}}\cong R\Gamma_{c}(\mathfrak{S}_{K,\bar{\eta}},\Lambda)\cong R\Gamma_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},R\psi(\Lambda))\cong R\Gamma_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},\Lambda)

where the first isomorphism is proper base change, the second follows from the existence of toroidal compactifications (
[
Per19
]
), see
[
LS18
]
for example, and the last follows from the smoothness of
\mathfrak{S}_{K}
. We have thus proved the identification over the generic fiber.
∎

Corollary 7.11
.

We have
R^{i}\pi^{\diamond}_{!}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
is a locally constant sheaf whose stalks are identified with
H^{i}_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
.

We can now prove the
S=T
conjecture in
[
XZ17
]
.

Theorem 7.12
.

As operators on
H^{i}_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
,
the Hecke correspondence
T_{V}^{S}

is the same as the excursion correspondence
\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{W,*}S_{V}^{W}
, the analytification of the excursion operator in
[
XZ17
]
(whose action on
H^{i}_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
is the same as the action as defined in
[
XZ17
]
through comparison of cohomology of v-sheaves and perfect schemes in
[
Sch17
, Section 27]
).

Proof.

Recall that we can pushforward cohomological correspondence (
[
XZ17
, A.2.6]
), so we can pushforward (
48
) along
R(\pi^{\diamond})_{!}
to obtain

R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond})_{!}\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{*}S_{V}:R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond})_{!}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}\longrightarrow R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond})_{!}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}

which is a global section of the internal hom
\mathcal{H}\text{om}(R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond})_{!}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell},R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond})_{!}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
. Since
R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond})_{!}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
is locally constant, so is

\mathcal{H}\text{om}(R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond})_{!}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell},R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond})_{!}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
,
and we see that

R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond})_{!}\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{*}S_{V}

is constant. In other words, for any geometric point
\bar{\eta}
of
\operatorname{Spd}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, we have that the restriction of
R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond})_{!}\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{*}S_{V}
to
\bar{\eta}
,
which is nothing but

R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond}_{\bar{\eta}})_{!}\operatorname{loc}_{p,\bar{\eta}}^{*}S_{V,\bar{\eta}}:H^{i}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{K,\bar{\eta}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})\longrightarrow H^{i}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{K,\bar{\eta}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}),

is identified with its restriction to the special fiber

R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond}_{s})_{!}\operatorname{loc}_{p,s}^{*}S_{V,s}:H^{i}_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})\longrightarrow H^{i}_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})

under the canonical identification between
H^{i}_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})

and
H^{i}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{K,\bar{\eta}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
, where
s:=\overline{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
.

Now theorem
7.8
tells that
\operatorname{loc}_{p,\bar{\eta}}^{*}S_{V,\bar{\eta}}=T_{V}^{S}
is the Hecke operator, so the action

R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond}_{s})_{!}\operatorname{loc}_{p,s}^{*}S_{V,s}

is identified with the Hecke operator on
H^{i}_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
. Recall that Hecke operators on

H^{i}_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})

is defined through the identification of

H^{i}_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})

with

H^{i}_{c}(\mathcal{X}_{K,\bar{\eta}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})

and the Hecke action on the latter, which is exactly the way we identify the excursion operators.

It remains to identify

R^{i}(\pi^{\diamond}_{s})_{!}\operatorname{loc}_{p,s}^{*}S_{V,s}

with excursion operators defined by Xiao–Zhu.
As usual, we let

p-\operatorname{Isog}_{V}:=\underset{\nu}{\cup}\ p-\operatorname{Isog}_{\nu}

where
\nu
varies from heighest weights of irreducible summands of
V
.

We first observe that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p-\operatorname{Isog}_{V,s}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu,s}^{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{loc}_{p,s}^{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{loc}_{p,s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{loc}_{p,s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}

factorizes through

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p-\operatorname{Isog}_{V,s}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu}^{V,W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu|\mu,s}^{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sht}_{\mu,s},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{H,W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}

where
\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{W}

sends
A
to (the corresponding torsor) the Dieudonné module associated to
A[p^{\infty}]
together with Hodge tensors, and similarly for
\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{H,W}
. Up to truncation, the upper half diagram is precisely the analytification of the pullback diagram considered in
[
XZ17
, Section 7.3.11]
.
Then proposition
5.15
tells us that
\operatorname{loc}_{p,s}^{*}S_{V,s}

is the same as the pullback of
S_{V}^{W}

along the upper half of the diagram. Since
S_{V}^{W}
is the analytification of the truncated one (proposition
5.15
), and
\diamond
-analytification of cohomological correspondences commutes with pullback (proposition
A.4
),
we have that
\operatorname{loc}_{p,s}^{*}S_{V,s}
is exactly the analytification of the excursion operator on the special fiber of Shimura varieties constructed in
[
XZ17
]
(being defined as the pullback of the truncated version of
\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{W}
of the truncated
S_{V}^{W}
in the category of perfect schemes). Therefore, we have proved that the Hecke operator
T_{V}^{S}

on
H^{i}_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})

is the same as the action of
\operatorname{loc}_{p}^{W,*}S_{V}^{W}
.
∎

Corollary 7.13
.

(
[
XZ18
, Proposition 6.3.1]
)
The action of
T_{V}^{S}
on
H^{i}_{c}(\bar{\mathfrak{X}}_{K},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})

satisfies Eichler–Shimura relation.

Remark 7.14
.

The Eichler–Shimura relation, or the Blasius–Rogawski conjecture has been proved in many special cases previously, see
[
FC90
]

[
Wed00
]
,
[
Lee20
]
,

[
Bü02
]
,
and
[
Kos14
]

for example. The method adopted in most previous works is to study the algebraic correspondences explicitly, which is very different from our categorical approach.

Appendix A
Algebraic stacks and v-stacks

A.1.
Comparison of cohomology

By
[
Sch17
, Section 27]
, we can associate a v-sheaf
X^{\diamond\diamond}
to any scheme
X
locally of finite type over a complete discrete valuation ring
\mathcal{O}
with perfect residue field of characteristic
p
.
X^{\diamond\diamond}

is defined by sending characteristic
p
affinoid perfectoid
S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})

to the set of untilts
S^{\sharp}
over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O})
together with maps
S^{\sharp}\longrightarrow X
of locally ringed spaces over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O})
. Another way to view
X^{\diamond\diamond}
is that
X^{\diamond\diamond}
is the sheafification of the presheaf sending
S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
to the set of untilts
S^{\sharp}=\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp},R^{\sharp,+})
over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O})
together with maps
\operatorname{Spec}(R^{\sharp})\longrightarrow X
of schemes over
\mathcal{O}
.

On the other hand, there is another v-sheaf
X^{\diamond}
we can associate to any scheme
X
locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
.
X^{\diamond}
is defined to be the sheafification of the presheaf sending a characteristic
p
affinoid perfectoid space
S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
to the set of untilts
S^{\sharp}
together with morphisms
\operatorname{Spec}(R^{\sharp,+})\rightarrow X
of schemes over
\mathcal{O}
. In other words, it is the v-sheaf associated to the formal scheme
X^{\wedge}
, the completion of
X
along the special fiber of
\mathcal{O}
. We note that there is a
canonical map

a_{X}:X^{\diamond}\longrightarrow X^{\diamond\diamond}

sending the map
\operatorname{Spec}(R^{+})\rightarrow X
to its restriction on
\operatorname{Spec}(R)
, which is an open immersion. It is an isomorphism when
X
is proper over
\mathcal{O}
.

We extend the construction to algebraic stacks locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
. For clarity, we specify our convention on algebraic stacks to be fppf sheaves of groupoids with diagonals representable by algebraic spaces, which admit representable smooth surjective maps from schemes. Let
X
be such an algebraic stack, we define
X^{\diamond}
to be the stackification of the prestack sending
S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
to the groupoid of untilts
S^{\sharp}=\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp},R^{\sharp,+})
of
S
together with morphisms of stacks
\operatorname{Spec}(R^{\sharp,+})\rightarrow X
over
\mathcal{O}
. Similarly, we define
X^{\diamond\diamond}
to be the stackification of the prestack sending
S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
to the groupoid of untilts
S^{\sharp}=\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp},R^{\sharp,+})
of
S
together with morphisms of stacks
\operatorname{Spec}(R^{\sharp})\rightarrow X
over
\mathcal{O}
. Then as usual, there is a canonical map

a_{X}:X^{\diamond}\longrightarrow X^{\diamond\diamond}.

We recall that when
X
is a scheme locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, there is a natural morphism

c_{X}:X^{\diamond\diamond}_{v}\rightarrow X_{\text{ét}}

from the v-site of
X^{\diamond\diamond}
to the étale site of
X
, which is defined by sending a scheme
U
together with a smooth morphism
U\rightarrow X

to
U^{\diamond\diamond}\rightarrow X^{\diamond\diamond}
, see
[
Sch17
, Section 27]
. Let
\Lambda
be a finite ring killed by
N
with
p
not dividing
N
, then

c_{X}
induces a functor

c_{X}^{*}:D(X_{\text{ét}},\Lambda)\rightarrow D_{\text{ét}}(X^{\diamond\diamond},\Lambda),

and for any
f:X\rightarrow Y
separated, there is a canonical identification

c^{*}_{Y}Rf_{!}\cong Rf_{!}^{\diamond\diamond}c_{X}^{*},

see
[
Sch17
, Proposition 27.5]
. Moreover, we have a canonical identification

f^{\diamond\diamond,*}c_{Y}^{*}\cong c_{X}^{*}f^{*}.

For a general algebraic stack
X
locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, we can choose a smooth surjective map
U\rightarrow X
with
U
being a scheme, then it is well-known
(see
[
LZ14
, Corollary 5.3.6]
for example)
that we have a canonical identification

\mathcal{D}(X,\Lambda)\cong\text{lim}\ \mathcal{D}(U^{\bullet}_{\text{ét}},\Lambda)

where
U^{\bullet}
is the Čech nerve of
U
,
\mathcal{D}(X,\Lambda)
is the
\infty
-categorical enhancement of the derived category of the lisse-étale site of
X
and similarly for the right hand side. We now can extend the comparison morphism to algebraic stacks

c_{X}^{*}:\mathcal{D}(X,\Lambda)\cong\text{lim}\ \mathcal{D}(U^{\bullet}_{\text{ét}},\Lambda)\overset{\text{lim}\ c_{U^{\bullet}}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}\text{lim}\ \mathcal{D}_{\text{ét}}(U^{\bullet,\diamond\diamond},\Lambda)\cong\mathcal{D}_{\text{ét}}(X^{\diamond\diamond},\Lambda),

where the last isomorphism is
[
Sch17
, Proposition 17.3]
and remark 17.4 (and the commutation of
⋄⋄
with fiber products). It is not hard to see that
c_{X}^{*}
is independent of the choice of
U
.

Moreover, given a separated representable map
f:X\rightarrow Y
between algebraic stacks locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, we choose a smooth surjective map
U\rightarrow Y
from a scheme
U
, and let
V
be the pullback of
U
along
f
with the induced map
g:V\rightarrow U
. Then
g
extends to
g^{\bullet}:V^{\bullet}\rightarrow U^{\bullet}
, and the limit of

c^{*}_{U^{\bullet}}Rg^{\bullet}_{!}\cong R(g^{\bullet,\diamond\diamond})_{!}c_{V^{\bullet}}^{*}

tells us that

c^{*}_{Y}Rf_{!}\cong Rf_{!}^{\diamond\diamond}c_{X}^{*}.

Similarly, we have

f^{\diamond\diamond,*}c_{Y}^{*}\cong c_{X}^{*}f^{*}.

In summary, we have the following theorem.

Theorem A.1
.

Let
\Lambda
be a finite ring killed by
N
with
p
not dividing
N
,
X
an algebraic stack locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, then we have a canonical comparison map

c_{X}^{*}:\mathcal{D}(X,\Lambda)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\text{ét}}(X^{\diamond\diamond},\Lambda).

Moreover, for any
f:X\rightarrow Y
representable separated map, there are canonical identifications

c^{*}_{Y}Rf_{!}\cong Rf_{!}^{\diamond\diamond}c_{X}^{*}

and

f^{\diamond\diamond,*}c_{Y}^{*}\cong c_{X}^{*}f^{*}.

Lastly, we pass to adic sheaves. Let
l
be a prime different from
p
, and
\Lambda
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
, we know by
[
LZ14
]
that

\mathcal{D}(X,\Lambda)=\underset{n}{\text{lim}}\ \mathcal{D}(X,\Lambda/l^{n}),

where the limit is taken as
\infty
-categories. On the other hand, we also have

\mathcal{D}_{\text{ét}}(X^{\diamond\diamond},\Lambda)=\underset{n}{\text{lim}}\ \mathcal{D}_{\text{ét}}(X^{\diamond\diamond},\Lambda/l^{n})

by
[
Sch17
, Proposition 26.2]
. The analogue of theorem
A.1
for such
\Lambda
follows directly from the limit expression, using that pullback and
!
-pushforward are compatible with the limit expression, see
[
Sch17
, Remark 26.3]
. Further, we can (idempotently completely) invert
l
and taking the limit over finite extensions of
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
to obtain the comparison with coefficient
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
.

Remark A.2
.

If the algebraic stack
X
is locally of finite type over a perfect field, then
X^{\diamond\diamond}
depends only on the perfection
X^{\operatorname{perf}}
of
X
, and theorem
A.1
holds for
X^{\operatorname{perf}}
since perfection does not change cohomology. This is an important case in this article.

A natural question is whether we have a similar comparison result for the
\diamond
-analytification. The answer is no in general, but it holds in the following special case.

Proposition A.3
.

Let
X
be a scheme locally of finite type over a perfect field
k
, and
\Lambda
be either a finite extension of
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
or
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
then we have a canonical identification

R\pi_{!}^{\diamond}\Lambda\cong c_{k}^{*}R\pi_{!}\Lambda

where
\pi:X\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(k)

is the structure map.

Proof.

It is enough to show the result after base change to the algebraically closure of
k
, so we assume that
k
is algebraically closed, and we are aiming to prove

R\Gamma_{c}(X^{\diamond},\Lambda)=R\Gamma_{c}(X,\Lambda).

By a standard Čech cohomology argument, we can assume that
X=\operatorname{Spec}(R)
is affine. Moreover, it is enough to prove the case when
\Lambda
is finite, so we assume that.

Let
C
be a completed algebraically closure of
k((t))
, and
by proper base change (
[
Sch17
, Proposition 22.15]
), we have

R\Gamma_{c}(X^{\diamond},\Lambda)=R\Gamma_{c}(X^{\diamond}_{C},\Lambda)

where

X^{\diamond}_{C}:=X^{\diamond}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}(k)}\operatorname{Spd}(C)
. We observe that

X^{\diamond}_{k((t))}=\operatorname{Spd}(R((t)),R[[t]])

where
X^{\diamond}_{k((t))}:=X^{\diamond}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}(k)}\operatorname{Spd}(k((t)),k[[t]])
, which visibly is the analytification of the analytic adic space
X^{\operatorname{ad}}_{k((t))}:=\operatorname{Spa}(R((t)),R[[t]])
. Then by
[
Sch17
, Lemma 15.6]
,

R\Gamma_{c}(X^{\diamond}_{C},\Lambda)=R\Gamma_{c}((X^{\operatorname{ad}}_{k((t))})_{C},\Lambda).

To compute

R\Gamma_{c}((X^{\operatorname{ad}}_{k((t))})_{C},\Lambda)
, we observe that

\operatorname{Spa}(R((t)),R[[t]])=\operatorname{Spa}(R[[t]],R[[t]])_{\eta}:=\operatorname{Spa}(R[[t]],R[[t]])\times_{\operatorname{Spa}(k[[t]],k[[t]])}\operatorname{Spa}(k((t)),k[[t]]),

namely,
X^{\operatorname{ad}}_{k((t))}
is the generic fiber of the formal scheme
\mathfrak{X}:=\operatorname{Spa}(R[[t]],R[[t]])

over
\operatorname{Spa}(k[[t]],k[[t]])
. Note that
\mathfrak{X}
is the
t
-adic completion of the scheme
\operatorname{Spec}(R)\times_{\operatorname{Spec}(k)}\operatorname{Spec}(k[[t]])
over
\operatorname{Spec}(k[[t]])
, which has special fiber
X
.
Then
[
Hub13
, Theorem 5.7.8]
tells us that

R\Gamma_{c}((X^{\operatorname{ad}}_{k((t))})_{C},\Lambda)=R\Gamma_{c}(X,R\psi(\Lambda))

where
R\psi
is the nearby cycle functor with respect to
\operatorname{Spec}(R[[t]])
over
\operatorname{Spec}(k[[t]])
. Since
\operatorname{Spec}(R)\times_{\operatorname{Spec}(k)}\operatorname{Spec}(k[[t]])

is a constant family over
\operatorname{Spec}(k[[t]])
, we have
R\psi(\Lambda)=\Lambda
, finishing the proof.

∎

A.2.
Comparison of cohomological correspondences

We have established the comparison of cohomology, and now we want to use it to compare cohomological correspondences. Let

\mathscr{C}:s^{*}\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow t^{!}\mathcal{G}

be a cohomological correspondence supported on

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

where
X,Z
and
Y
are algebraic stacks locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}
, or perfections of such stacks over a perfect field,
s
and
t
are representable separated morphisms, and
\mathcal{F}\in D^{b}(X_{\text{ét}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
(resp.
\mathcal{G}\in D^{b}(Y_{\text{ét}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
). There is a natural analytification of
\mathscr{C}
,

\mathscr{C}^{\diamond\diamond}:(X^{\diamond\diamond},c_{X}^{*}\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow(Y^{\diamond\diamond},c_{Y}^{*}\mathcal{G})

supported on

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\diamond\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\diamond\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\diamond\diamond},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\diamond\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{\diamond\diamond}}

which is defined by

\mathscr{C}^{\diamond\diamond}:s^{\diamond\diamond,*}c_{X}^{*}\mathcal{F}\cong c_{Z}^{*}s^{*}\mathcal{F}\overset{c_{Z}^{*}\mathscr{C}}{\longrightarrow}c_{Z}^{*}t^{!}\mathcal{G}\rightarrow t^{\diamond\diamond,!}c_{Y}^{*}\mathcal{G},

where the last map is the adjoint of the canonical map

t^{\diamond\diamond}_{!}c_{Z}^{*}t^{!}\mathcal{G}\cong c_{Y}^{*}t_{!}t^{!}\mathcal{G}\rightarrow c_{Y}^{*}\mathcal{G}

with the first isomorphism being theorem
A.1
and the second the counit map.

This gives the
\diamond\diamond
-analytification of the cohomological correspondence, but what we need in this article is the analytification with respect to
\diamond
. We can construct the desired cohomological correspondence from
\mathscr{C}^{\diamond\diamond}
under additional hypothesis. We have naturally a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\diamond\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\diamond\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\diamond\diamond},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\diamond\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{\diamond\diamond}}

assume the right square is Cartesian,
11
11

11

As noted by the referee, being Cartesian fails for open immersions such as
\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
then we can pullback
\mathscr{C}^{\diamond\diamond}
along it to have the cohomological correspondence

\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}:(X^{\diamond},a_{X}^{*}c_{X}^{*}\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow(Y^{\diamond},a_{Y}^{*}c_{Y}^{*}\mathcal{G})

supported on
Z^{\diamond}
.

We record some formal properties of
\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}
.

Proposition A.4
.

\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}
commutes with composition and pullbacks. More precisely, if
\mathscr{C}_{1}
and
\mathscr{C}_{2}
are composable, and assume that the right squares involving
a_{Z}
and
a_{Y}
of both
\mathscr{C}_{1}
and
\mathscr{C}_{2}
are Cartesian, then so does
\mathscr{C}_{1}\circ\mathscr{C}_{2}
, and we have

\mathscr{C}_{1}^{\diamond}\circ\mathscr{C}_{2}^{\diamond}=(\mathscr{C}_{1}\circ\mathscr{C}_{2})^{\diamond}.

Given a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{2},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{2}}

and a cohomological correspondence

\mathscr{C}_{2}:(X_{2},\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow(Y_{2},\mathcal{G})

supported on
Z_{2}
,
suppose that either the vertical arrows are smooth or the right square is Cartesian, so we can pullback
\mathscr{C}_{2}
along the diagram to obtain
\mathscr{C}_{1}
supported on
Z_{1}
. Assume that both
\mathscr{C}_{1}^{\diamond}
and
\mathscr{C}_{2}^{\diamond}
exists, i.e. the square corresponding to
a_{Y_{1}}
and
a_{Z_{1}}
(resp.
a_{Y_{2}}
and
a_{Z_{2}}
) is Cartesian, then
\mathscr{C}_{1}^{\diamond}
is the pullback of
\mathscr{C}_{2}^{\diamond}
along the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{1}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{1}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{2}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{2}^{\diamond}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{2}^{\diamond}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b^{\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}^{\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{1}^{\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{2}^{\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{2}^{\diamond}}

A.3.
Truncated moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas and truncated Witt vector Hecke stacks

We would like to compare the cohomological correspondences in
[
XZ17
]
between moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas with the ones between v-stacks. More precisely, only the truncated moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas are considered in
[
XZ17
]
since the untruncated ones are not algebraic stacks and the cohomological formalism does not directly apply to them.
However, the problem disappears by passing to v-stacks as the formalism developed in
[
Sch17
]
is very general and does apply to stacks with infinite dimensional automorphisms.

We recall the definition of truncated Witt vector Hecke stacks and moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas in
[
XZ17
]
. We omit the superscript
W
for moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas, as we will only use the truncated version for Witt vector ones. More importantly, we use the superscript
W
for the untruncated moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas or Hecke stacks viewed as v-stacks, whereas the truncated ones below are only defined as perfect stacks.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, and
L^{m}G
be the perfect scheme defined by
L^{m}G(R)=G(W_{m}(R))
(with
W_{\infty}(R)=W(R)
) for any perfect algebra
R
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
. Suppose now that
m
is large with respect to
\mu_{\bullet}
so that the action of
LG(R)
on
Gr^{W}_{\mu_{\bullet}}
factorizes through
L^{m}G
, where
Gr_{\mu_{\bullet}}^{W}
is the (twisted products of) Witt vector affine Grassmannian, see
[
XZ17
, Definition 5.1.2]
. Let

\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{loc}(m)}_{\mu_{\bullet}}:=L^{m}G\setminus Gr^{W}_{\mu_{\bullet}}.

We observe that

\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{loc}(\infty),\diamond}_{\mu_{\bullet}}=\operatorname{Hecke}^{W}_{\mu_{\bullet}}

and

\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{loc}(\infty),\diamond\diamond}_{\mu_{\bullet}}=\operatorname{Hecke}_{\mu_{\bullet},s}^{\operatorname{loc},(\{1\},\cdots,\{n\})}.

Let
(m,n)
be a pair of non-negative integers such that
m-n
is
\mu_{\bullet}
-large, see
[
XZ17
, Definition 3.1.6]
, and

\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}_{\mu_{\bullet}}

be defined as in
[
XZ17
, Definition 5.3.1]
, and similarly for

\operatorname{Sht}^{0,\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}_{\mu_{\bullet}|\nu_{\bullet}}
. By
loc.cit.
section 5.3.2, both

\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}_{\mu_{\bullet}}

and

\operatorname{Sht}^{0,\operatorname{loc}(m,n)}_{\mu_{\bullet}|\nu_{\bullet}}

can be written as quotients by
L^{m}G
of perfect schemes perfectly of finite type, so they fit into the cohomological formalism of the previous section by remark
A.2
. Now by
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.4.1]
, we have canonical identifications

D_{\text{ét}}(\operatorname{Sht}^{W}_{\mu_{\bullet}},\Lambda)\cong D_{\text{ét}}(\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond}_{\mu_{\bullet}},\Lambda),

D_{\text{ét}}(\operatorname{Sht}^{0,W}_{\mu_{\bullet}|\nu_{\bullet}},\Lambda)\cong D_{\text{ét}}(\operatorname{Sht}^{0,\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond}_{\mu_{\bullet}|\nu_{\bullet}},\Lambda)

and

D_{\text{ét}}(\operatorname{Hecke}^{W}_{\mu_{\bullet}},\Lambda)\cong D_{\text{ét}}(\operatorname{Hecke}^{\operatorname{loc}(m),\diamond}_{\mu_{\bullet}},\Lambda).

More precisely, the conclusion of
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.4.1]
holds if we change the assumption to that the group
H
has a filtration with graded pieces being extensions of closed unit discs (instead of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
) v-locally, which the analytification
L^{m}G^{\diamond}
does, and the proof in
cit.loc.
goes without change.
In other words, the truncated moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas and Witt vector Hecke stacks have the same cohomology with the untruncated ones. Similar identifications hold between moduli spaces of local Witt vector shtukas or Witt vector Hecke stacks and
\diamond\diamond
-analytification of truncated ones, for example

D_{\text{ét}}(\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc},W}_{\mu_{\bullet}},\Lambda)\cong D_{\text{ét}}(\operatorname{Sht}^{\operatorname{loc}(m,n),\diamond\diamond}_{\mu_{\bullet}},\Lambda).

Remark A.5
.

The introduction of truncated moduli spaces of Witt vector shtukas or Witt vector Hecke stacks in
[
XZ17
]
is because the untruncated ones do not directly fit into the cohomological formalism of Artin stacks. Using the new cohomology theory of v-stacks developed in
[
Sch17
]
, we believe large parts of
[
XZ17
]
can be simplified using the untruncated objects, with some caution on the arguments using fixed points results.
